  4. founder_indian_company — Vikram Rao, US resident owning 100% of an Indian Pvt Ltd. cfc / Form 5471, a partial share buyback from his own company (deemed_dividend_buyback_mismatch), a brought-forward STCG loss bigger than this year's STCG gain (the "partially set off" state of loss set-off), and a dividend treaty election on file at 25% (worse than the 20% domestic rate) with TRC/Form 10F present — computation correctly ignores the election since domestic is more beneficial (s.90(2) protection, not just "not applied").
  5. us_citizen_expat_india — Grace Thomas, US citizen living in India. FEIE + PFIC (citizenship-based taxation), a gift from her father — a long-term green-card holder who relinquished it and was found to be a covered expatriate (foreign_gift_3520, covered_expat_gift_tax), and a taxable NPS withdrawal (the NPS half of the EPF/NPS US-income wiring, distinct from Aarav's EPF-interest case).
  6. india_pvt_ltd — Business POV: Indian domestic company, §115BAA, ITR-6.
  7. us_ccorp_indian_sub — Business POV: Delaware C-Corp with an Indian subsidiary; GILTI.
  8. foreign_holdco_poem_india — Business POV: foreign-incorporated (Singapore) holding company whose Place of Effective Management facts resolve it to an Indian tax resident anyway (entity_dual_residency_poem) — entity-level dual residency with no individual-style tie-breaker.
  9. sharma_huf — Business POV: an HUF managing ancestral property and FD investments in India. Control-and-management residency test (not day-count, not POEM). Sits right at the §87A rebate threshold — demonstrates the entity-aware fix that HUF is not entitled to the individual-only rebate. PAN also unlinked from Aadhaar (pan_not_linked_aadhaar).

Coverage guarantee: every finding id emitted by detectConflicts() in conflicts.js fires in at least one of the 9 profiles — verified by running WISING.analyze() against all 9 and diffing the union of triggered ids against every add(id, ...) call site in the source. When a new finding is added to conflicts.js, extend the existing profile whose persona it fits most naturally (don't reach for a synthetic kitchen-sink profile) so this stays true — otherwise there is no one-click way to verify the new finding actually renders.

Part H — Honest limitations to keep stating

  • Planning-grade tables (FY2026-27 / TY2026), not a filing engine.
  • The engine trusts the Layer 1 forms' final residency status rather than

re-deriving it from the determination inputs.

  • FX is a flat anchor; statutory FTC needs per-transaction TT rates.
  • ~~Until Phases 1–4 land, multi-entity numbers are flattened into the individual~~

— stale as of the Business tab shipping; entities show separately (not flattened), just not yet as a traceable graph. See docs/BUSINESS_ENTITY_ARCHITECTURE.md for the current, accurate state.

  • US-state rows in the Monitor are illustrative (state tax computation not built — gap tracker US-12).

Business & Multi-Entity Architecture — Spec

Supersedes Part F/G of COVERAGE_AND_ARCHITECTURE.md. Those sections described an entity-graph architecture that was never built; what shipped instead (BusinessView.jsx, a flat entity list) is simpler and the two were never reconciled. This document replaces both with a spec grounded in what Layer 1 actually collects today (verified by direct grep against layer1_india.html / layer1_us.html, not recalled from the old plan) and a phased build order.

Directive this doc executes on: "We need multi-entity architecture and a thorough spec for the business entities — we cannot keep it shallow at all."

India and US are both first-class jurisdictions in this spec. An earlier version of this document structured itself India-first — four fully-numbered India subsections (§2.1-2.4) followed by two terser US subsections tacked on after (§2.5-2.6), a "Phase 1" that was India-only with US depreciation demoted to a lettered "Phase 1b" satellite, and an entire concern (residency/entity-type determination) that existed for both countries in the live engine but had no section in this doc at all. Restructured (this pass) so every concern gets one section with India and US presented as equal-weight, side-by-side tracks — including two concerns (residency/entity-type determination, conflict detection, return-form determination) that were previously undocumented here even though they're real, load-bearing parts of "business entity architecture" and were shipped separately, tracked only as scattered GAP_TRACKER.md rows with no structural home in this spec.

0. The finding that reframes everything

India business/firm/company computation is not "shallow" — it was, at the time this doc was first written, non-functional against real Layer 1 data. Since fixed (Phase 0, below).

normalize.js read b.net_profit_inr off every business_entries[] item to build both the business-income aggregate and the entity tax base (computeIndiaEntityTax, used for company 22%/25%/15%+MAT and firm/LLP flat-30% computation alike).

net_profit_inr does not exist anywhere in layer1_india.html. Grep across the entire 18,000+-line form for that field name returns zero matches. The form collects turnover, receipts (digital/cash split), a full expense-category breakdown, depreciation asset blocks, F&O/speculative splits, partner-firm remuneration, MSME payment timing — and never computes or exports a net-profit summary.

The only place net_profit_inr was ever set was hand-authored demo data in engine/profiles.js — every business/company/firm demo profile worked only because it cheated past a computation gap that would have zeroed out any real filer's business income.

The exact same shape of bug existed on the US side, not a smaller afterthought of it: partnerships_k1[].guaranteed_payments_usd was (and Box 14A alongside it) fully collected by Layer 1 US but read nowhere in normalize.js — not into businessUs, not into SE-tax base, not into QBI exclusion. A general partner's guaranteed payments (SE-tax-subject, QBI-ineligible, unlike ordinary K-1 income) were silently dropped from income entirely.

Phase 0 fixed both of these together, in the same pass, because every other phase's numbers are meaningless until the base computation actually runs — for either country.

1. What Layer 1 already collects, by entity type

Verified directly against the two forms — this is the actual current data model, not the old plan's sketch.

1.1 Entity classification & residency-determination fields

Both sides collect the raw facts that decide what kind of taxpayer this is and which country's residency test applies — this is the data §2 (below) is built on, and it was entirely missing from earlier versions of this table even though it's exactly as load-bearing as the income fields in §1.2.

IndiaUS
Entity typeprofile.entity_type: individual \huf \firm \llp \company \trust \ngo \society \political_party \aop \boi \ajp \localprofile.tax_entity_type: individual \ccorp \scorp \partnership \trust \llc (→ profile.llc_tax_election resolves the LLC's actual federal classification)
Residency test — company/entityresidency_detail.is_indian_company (incorporation, s.6(3) — unconditional if true); when false, company_residency{}: board_meetings_primarily_outside_india, key_management_location, directors_in_india_count/directors_outside_india_count (POEM facts)profile.incorporated_in_us / profile.incorporation_state — place of organization (IRC §7701(a)(4)), collected only when tax_entity_type is ccorp/scorp/partnership/trust
Residency test — HUF/firm/LLP/AOP/BOI/trust/etcresidency_detail.is_wholly_outside_india (s.6(2)/s.6(4) control-and-management test)(no equivalent entity kind on the US side — a US trust uses the same incorporation-style organizational test as the row above, not a separate control-and-management concept)
Cross-border scoperouter.jurisdiction (dual/single_india/single_us, Layer 0), router.us_days/is_us_citizen/has_green_card/has_us_source_income_or_assets(same router object — the fields above ARE the US-side scope signal; there's no separate India-side "has_india_source_income_or_assets" field since India is this tool's base jurisdiction)

profile.tax_entity_type (individual/ccorp/scorp/partnership/trust) plus profile.llc_tax_election drive computeUsEntityTax's routing (C-corp 21% flat vs S-corp/partnership pass-through) and entity.usReturnForm (§4, below).

2. Residency & entity-type determination

Was entirely undocumented in this spec even though it's exactly as load-bearing as §3's income computation — a taxpayer's residency status decides which country's rate schedule and worldwide-vs-source basis applies at all, before any net-profit number matters. Shipped this pass (gap tracker IN-34, XB-18, XB-19).

2.1 India

  • Company: residency_detail.is_indian_company === true → unconditionally resident (s.6(3), place of incorporation controls, no POEM test reached). When false, residency instead turns on Place of Effective Management — company_residency{}'s board-meeting location, key-management location, and director in-India/outside-India split. A foreign-incorporated company whose POEM facts point to India is resident here without ceasing to be resident wherever it's actually incorporated — a genuine entity-level dual residency with no individual-style Article 4 tie-breaker to mechanically resolve it (Article 4(3) sends companies to competent-authority mutual agreement instead). Domestic-incorporated status also separately gates which rate schedule applies (§3.7) — incorporation controls residency, but is not the only thing that controls the rate schedule, and conflating the two was the exact bug IN-34 fixed.
  • HUF / firm / LLP / AOP / BOI / trust / etc.: a different, qualitative test (s.6(2)/s.6(4)) — resident unless control and management of its affairs is wholly outside India (residency_detail.is_wholly_outside_india). Not a day-count test, not POEM — a third distinct residency mechanism from the two above. HUF carries one further wrinkle: once the HUF itself is resident, ROR-vs-RNOR sub-status (s.6(6)(b)) still turns on the karta's own individual day-count, separately from the HUF's own control-and-management determination.

2.2 US

  • ccorp / scorp / partnership / trust: domestic status by place of organization/incorporation (IRC §7701(a)(4) for corporations; analogous organized-under-state-law tests for the others) — profile.incorporated_in_us / incorporation_state. Never a day-count/presence test — Substantial Presence (§7701(b)) is an individual-only concept and does not apply to an entity at all, regardless of how many days anyone associated with it spends in the US.
  • When tax_entity_type is left at its individual default because no separate US entity was ever organized for this taxpayer (common for an India-only company/HUF/firm with zero US nexus), that absence of a ccorp/scorp/partnership/trust election is itself the fact: the taxpayer is foreign to the US by default, with no US-side residency test to run at all — not "an individual who happens to score 0 days."

2.3 Cross-border jurisdiction scope

A taxpayer can be exposed to only one of the two countries, and the Monitor now reflects that dynamically rather than always rendering a dual-country picture:

  • model.meta.hasIndiaScope / hasUsScope are derived from what was actually reported — router.us_days > 0, is_us_citizen, has_green_card, has_us_source_income_or_assets — not from whether Layer 1's india/us object merely exists in the bundle (both always do, as a shell, so neither Layer 1 form crashes if opened) and not from Router's own jurisdiction toggle alone (a real preparer can leave that on "Dual" out of habit while never entering a single US fact). Router's own explicit "India only"/"US only" selection still wins outright when a preparer deliberately sets it.
  • When a taxpayer has no real scope on one side (e.g. an India-incorporated company with zero US presence), the Monitor's header entity badge, residency Flag cards, Residency Determination panel entries, and DTAA Treaty Position card (Article 4 tie-breaker / TRC / Form 10F / PE / 1040-NR are all meaningless without real dual exposure) now render only the in-scope country — instead of fabricating a "US: Individual" badge or a 0/183-day Substantial Presence bar for a taxpayer with no US nexus at all.

3. Income / net-profit computation depth per entity

This is the bulk of the original "not shallow" ask, and (critically) almost none of it needs new Layer 1 fields — it's engine computation against data that already exists. Each concern below is one section with India and US presented side by side, rather than all India concerns followed by all US concerns.

3.1 Net profit / entity-level taxable income

India — real net profit per business_entries[] item (replaces the phantom net_profit_inr field, §0):

if presumptive_scheme selected:
    44AD-equivalent (s.58 table): rate = 6% on digital_receipts, 8% on cash_receipts
    44ADA-equivalent (professionals): rate = 50% on gross_receipts (ada_* split)
    44AE-equivalent (goods carriages): per-vehicle deemed income × vehicle count
else (regular books):
    net_profit = gross_receipts
               − Σ(expenses{} deductible categories)
               − current-year depreciation (§3.4)
               + s41_remission_income + s41_bad_debt_recovery
               − disallowances: s.40A(3) cash-payment limit, s.40(a)(i)/(ia)
                 non-TDS payments, s.43B(h) unpaid MSME dues
               − F&O/speculative kept OUT of this figure (§3.2)

Threshold tests captured and enforced: presumptive turnover caps (₹2cr/₹3cr business, ₹50L/₹75L professional, gated on <5% cash receipts under ITA 2025 s.58), and the 5-year re-entry lock-in (s44AD_last_exit_ay). Company rate-schedule selection (s.115BAB 15% / s.115BAA 22% / s.115BA 25% flat / default turnover-gated 25%-or-30%) is gated to domestic-incorporated companies only (§2.1) — a foreign-incorporated company resident via POEM computes under the separate foreign-company schedule (35% flat, lower 2%/5% surcharge tiers, MAT-exempt absent an India PE) regardless of any 115BAA/BAB/BA election on file.

US — two fixes, both data-complete:

  • partnerships_k1[].guaranteed_payments_usd (and Box 14A alongside it) fold into businessUs income and the SE-tax base — a general partner's guaranteed payments ARE self-employment income — but are explicitly excluded from QBI (§199A carves guaranteed payments out by statute; this must not follow the same qbiIncome += line as ordinary K-1 income, or QBI would be overstated).
  • c_corporations_1120[]'s own taxable income is derived from Schedule M-1 (corporate_financials.schedule_m1{}: net income per books + federal tax expense + 50%-meals-disallowed + foreign-taxes-deducted-not-credited + interest-expense-limitation + other additions − tax-exempt interest − tax depreciation in excess of book − other subtractions — the real Form 1120 Line 1-10 structure) whenever Layer 1 US actually collected one, falling back to business_income_usd only when it didn't (trusts excluded — Form 1041 doesn't carry Schedule M-1, matching Layer 1's own gate).

3.2 Character/type separation of trading income

India — F&O vs. speculative, kept genuinely separate per Layer 1's own (correct) split:

  • F&O (fno_turnover_inr, non_speculative_income_inr): ordinary PGBP income, added into the same net-profit pool as §3.1, eligible for tax-audit-threshold testing on turnover.
  • Speculative (speculative_income_inr, speculative_turnover_inr): a ring-fenced bucket — can only be set off against speculative income/losses (s.113), never against ordinary business profit. Mirrors the VDA-never-loss-set-off pattern already built for crypto — same shape of rule, different head.

US — no direct equivalent is currently modeled. §1256 mark-to-market contract treatment and straddle rules (which would be the closest US analog to a speculative/ordinary split) are not captured by Layer 1 US or this engine today; noted here as an open question for a future pass rather than a tracked gap-tracker row, since it hasn't been verified with the same grep-first discipline as the rest of this document.

3.3 Pass-through / distributive-share income

India — partner-firm pass-through (partner_firms[]):

  • remuneration_from_entity_inr + interest_on_capital_from_entity_inr → taxable PGBP income to the partner (subject to the s.40(b) cap at the firm's own return, which this app doesn't prepare — the entered figure is trusted rather than re-derived).
  • profit_share_exempt_inr → genuinely exempt to the partner (already taxed at the firm level under s.10(2A)) — not added to taxable income, shown only for reconciliation.

US — K-1 aggregation across all three K-1 types: a shared helper folds interest/ordinary+qualified dividends/STCG/LTCG(+net s.1231 gain)/rental+royalty boxes from partnerships_k1[], s_corporations_k1[], and trusts_estates_k1[] into the same aggregate buckets their directly-held counterparts use, bridging the real field-name variants across the three forms (e.g. royalties_usd vs royalty_income_usd). trusts_estates_k1[] (previously entirely absent from businessEntities()) now feeds ordinary income into businessUs/QBI on the same footing as the other two K-1 types.

3.4 Depreciation

India — real s.32 WDV block-of-assets computation from asset_blocks[] (opening WDV + additions − sale, half-rate under the <180-day proviso), plus s.32(1)(iia) additional depreciation for new manufacturing/power-generation plant & machinery. Tracked as its own line (not folded silently into net profit) because unabsorbedDepreciationCf needs the current-year charge to flow into loss-set-off carryforward when it exceeds the year's business income.

US — real IRS Pub 946 multi-year MACRS tables (200%-DB half-year convention for 3/5/7-year property, 150%-DB for 15-year, straight-line mid-month for 27.5/39-year real property), current §179 figures ($2,560,000 cap / $4,090,000 phase-out, OBBBA TY2026), and 100% bonus depreciation (permanently restored by OBBBA for property placed in service after 19 Jan 2025) — with taxpayer-wide §179 aggregation across businesses (cap, phase-out reduction, active-income limitation, proportional scaling). Scoped to self_employment[].assets[] (Schedule C) and farming_schedule_f[].assets[] (Schedule F) — both fully computed, multi-year-aware (an asset placed in service in a prior year correctly looks up that year's MACRS table row, not year-1 every year), sharing ONE taxpayer-wide §179 aggregation pool (real law caps/phases out §179 across ALL of a taxpayer's directly-owned trades/businesses together, not per-array). Farm's own income computation had a deeper, previously-undiscovered bug on the way to this fix: net_profit_usd/gross_income_usd are phantom fields (never written by the live form, exactly like self-employment's own pre-fix bug) — real gross income is the sum of itemized_income{}'s 8 Schedule F line items (netted against the accrual-method cost-of-purchases/inventory swing when applicable), and farm income previously never reached actual taxable income at all, only the SE-tax base, and only via the phantom field. K-1/1120 asset rows remain deliberately out of scope, not just deprioritized: those entities' Box 1 ordinary income is already net of the entity's own regular/bonus depreciation (only §179 is separately stated at the box level, as sec179_deduction_usd, because IRC §179(d)(8) requires partner-level aggregate-income testing) — a second per-asset computation against a K-1 recipient's own copy of the entity's asset list would double-count against Box 1, a genuinely different situation from farm's direct gross-receipts/expenses derivation (gap tracker US-18, still partial for this reason, not fully open). Landed DAG-only (prototypes/graph-pilot/aggregateusincome-nodes.js + assets-nodes.js) — the classic engine/*.js is permanently frozen (docs/DAG_MIGRATION_TRACKER.md §J) and never hand-edited again, so this fix is an intentional, verified (200-iteration differential fuzz, 0 new divergences) divergence from the frozen engine's still-buggy farm behavior, not a parity gap.

3.5 Narrower presumptive / specified regimes

India — three real, fully-data-complete, lower-priority gaps: s.44BBB (foreign companies, civil construction/turnkey power projects, 10% of gross receipts deemed profit, no expense computation); s.35AD (100% capital-expenditure deduction for specified businesses — cold chain, warehousing, hospitals, hotels — an alternative to normal depreciation for that asset, not a supplement); s.115V tonnage tax (shipping companies — presumptive income from net tonnage × per-day rate schedule, bypassing §3.1's net-profit computation entirely for that entry when opted in).

US — no equivalent narrow-regime gap has been identified in Layer 1 US's collected fields.

3.6 Explicitly out of scope (confirmed, not just omitted)

  • Transfer pricing (s.92 / IRC §482) — a real conflict-detection finding now fires on the related-party ownership link (§4.3), but remains disclosure-only by design: no arm's-length-price computation, since that genuinely isn't rule-encodable at the depth an ALP study requires.
  • US S-corp reasonable-compensation testing, built-in-gains tax, accumulated E&P tracking — checked directly: none of the underlying data (shareholder wage-vs-distribution split, prior C-corp E&P balance, asset built-in-gain basis) exists anywhere in Layer 1 US. A data-collection gap, not a computation gap — not a candidate for this spec's phases until new fields exist.

4. Conflict detection for business entities

Was entirely undocumented in this spec. The engine's detectConflicts() (conflicts.js) raises several findings specific to business-entity taxpayers, distinct from the individual-taxpayer findings the rest of the app is built around.

4.1 India-side

  • entity_dual_residency_poem (S.WARNING) — fires when a company is on file as NOT incorporated in India (§2.1) yet still resolves to ROR via POEM: flags the entity-level dual-residency exposure (resident here under s.6(3), and likely still resident wherever it's actually incorporated, with no mechanical Article 4 tie-breaker for companies).
  • india_itr_form_mismatch (S.WARNING) — WISING's own independent ITR-form solver (computeIndiaItrForm, cross-checking income thresholds, residency, capital gains, foreign assets/income, crypto, house-property count, brought-forward losses, speculative/F&O income) is run unconditionally and compared against Layer 1's own persisted itr_recommendation.form; a disagreement is surfaced rather than silently picking one, since it usually points to stale Layer 1 state.

4.2 US-side

  • No dedicated US-only business-entity finding exists as its own tracked ID today. The equivalent rigor was applied as a direct field-coverage audit rather than a runtime finding: S-corp K-1 Box 1 ordinary income, the filer's-own-entity business_income_usd/Schedule M-1 bridge, and all three K-1 types' passive-income boxes were each verified against the real field names Layer 1 US actually writes (not the names the engine had been reading) and fixed at the computation layer (§3.1/§3.3) rather than surfaced as a runtime conflict — there was nothing to flag the taxpayer about, the bug was purely in which field name the engine happened to read.

4.3 Cross-border

  • transfer_pricing (S.WARNING) — fires on the same US-owns-≥10%-of-a-foreign/Indian-corporation ownership fact the CFC/Form 5471 check already establishes ("associated enterprises" under s.92); not gated on the US owner's personal residency, since s.92 binds the Indian entity's own return regardless. amountUsd: 0, no ALP computation, by design (§3.6) — points to Form 3CEB/Rule 10D/§482 and a specialist engagement rather than asserting a number this app can't compute.

5. Return-form / filing determination per entity

Was entirely undocumented in this spec, though a real solver already exists and ships on both sides today.

5.1 India

computeIndiaItrForm (computation.js) independently determines ITR-1/2/3/4/5/6 eligibility from residency, income composition, capital gains, foreign assets/income, crypto, house-property count, brought-forward losses, and speculative/F&O income — company entity type routes to ITR-6, firm/LLP/local-authority to ITR-5, individual/HUF to ITR-2/3/4 depending on income composition. Cross-checked against Layer 1's own frontend recommendation (§4.1's india_itr_form_mismatch finding) rather than trusted alone — three concrete bugs were found and NOT inherited from Layer 1's own client-side calculator (a desynced "has business income" checkbox, a dead singular partner-remuneration field name, and an unreachable director-status control).

5.2 US

model.entity.usReturnForm routes directly off profile.tax_entity_type: ccorp → 1120, scorp → 1120-S, partnership → 1065, trust → 1041; an individual filer routes to 1040-NR when Layer 1 US recorded nra_specific.files_form_1040nr, else the standard 1040. This drives both the Compliance Calendar's entity-aware filing due dates (1120/1040 coincide at Apr 15/Oct 15; 1120-S/1065/1041 carry their own earlier statutory dates) and the FTC form selection (Form 1118 for a C-corp filer vs. Form 1116 otherwise).

5.3 What's not yet built

Both §5.1 and §5.2 determine one return form per taxpayer — correct for today's flat entity list, but not yet "one filing determination per entity within a consolidated multi-entity structure" (e.g. a founder's own 1040 and their >10%-owned foreign corporation's own separate filing obligations, tracked today only as a disclosure flag, not a distinct entity with its own return-form determination). That depends on §6's entity graph existing first — tracked as Phase 7, below, not a near-term gap.

6. Entity graph — the architectural layer Part F originally asked for

Phase 5 shipped (25 Jul 2026): the schema below is real, built in assets-nodes.js's buildEntityGraph (model.assets.entityGraph), DAG-only (docs/DAG_MIGRATION_TRACKER.md §M). Genuinely new discovery made while building it, not assumed from the schema: a single taxpayer bundle can legitimately contain TWO distinct root-level entities with different names (e.g. us_ccorp_indian_sub — a US C-corp parent and its differently-named Indian subsidiary, both real, both with their own return) — collapsing them into one root, as an early draft did, silently duplicated the subsidiary (once as the root, once again as a foreign_corp with an edge pointing at itself). Fixed by detecting a name mismatch between India's and US's own primary-entity names and creating two roots (root_in/root_us) connected by the real ownership edge, rather than one. Phase 6 also shipped (25 Jul 2026): every edge now carries a real calc/source trace object (verified structurally across all 12 fixtures — no edge without one), and K-1 entities' previously-silent passive-income contribution (interest/dividend/capital-gain/rental/royalty) is now its own decomposed, traced edge rather than an anonymous slice of the taxpayer's overall totals. No frontend consumes this yet (Phase 8).

Per-entity computation (§3) must be correct before this layer means anything — an entity graph consolidating wrong numbers is worse than no graph. Sequenced after §3 for that reason. Applies equally to both jurisdictions — the schema below is not India- or US-shaped, it's a shared abstraction over both.

Entity {
  id, kind: "individual" | "in_huf" | "in_firm" | "in_llp" | "in_company"
          | "us_scorp" | "us_ccorp" | "us_partnership" | "us_llc" | "us_trust"
          | "foreign_corp",
  jurisdiction, returnForm,
  layer1Ref: { form, path },     // traceable back to the exact Layer 1 source
  income, deductions, tax        // per-entity computed block, §3's output
}

Edge {
  from, to, ownershipPct,
  flow: "k1_passthrough" | "dividend" | "gilti" | "subpart_f" | "partner_remuneration" | "salary"
}

Entity extraction sources (confirmed against §1's inventory):

  • India: profile.entity_type for the primary entity; each business_entries[] item with entity_type !== 'individual'; each partner_firms[] item (an edge INTO the individual, not a separate computed entity — the firm's own return isn't prepared here).
  • US: each partnerships_k1[] / s_corporations_k1[] / c_corporations_1120[] / trusts_estates_k1[] item; each foreign_entities.foreign_corporations[] item.

Flows to model as edges, not silent sums (this is the actual "not shallow" ask — every cross-entity dollar traceable to its source):

  • K-1 pass-through → owner's income. Phase 6 shipped (25 Jul 2026): the ordinary-income edge every K-1 entity already had (Phase 5) now carries a real calc trace (Box-by-box breakdown, not just a bare total); a genuinely NEW second edge (flow: "k1_passive_income") decomposes each K-1's own interest/dividend/capital-gain/rental/royalty contribution — previously summed taxpayer-wide with no way to say which entity produced which slice (verified: the decomposed per-entity amounts sum to no more than the taxpayer's overall interest/dividend/etc. totals, confirming the split doesn't invent or drop money, just attributes it).
  • GILTI/Subpart F inclusion, US person owning ≥10% of a foreign (or Indian) corporation, with the §962 election option — currently disclosure-only (gap tracker XB-14); needs CFC financial statement fields (E&P, QBAI, tested income) Layer 1 doesn't collect yet — this is the one piece of §6 genuinely blocked on new fields, not just engine work. The GILTI edge itself now carries a real trace (Phase 6), but the underlying inclusion amount is still the hand-entered estimate XB-14 describes, not a from-scratch computation.
  • Partner remuneration/interest/exempt-share (§3.3) as an edge from firm → partner — shipped (Phase 6), each with its own trace.

7. Frontend — per-entity views, not just the flat list

Phase 8, complete (25 Jul 2026) — all three items shipped, in sequence (data foundation → concrete per-entity view → cross-cutting nav), each verified live in a real browser (Playwright driver against monitor-next's dev server, not just a node script):

  • ✅ BusinessView drill-down, shipped: click "Entity graph detail" on any business/K-1/C-corp row → see that entity's own id/kind/jurisdiction/Layer 1 source path, plus every edge connected to it (Phase 6's traced edges), each independently expandable to its own calc/source breakdown. Building this surfaced a real, pre-existing gap, fixed in the same pass: India partner_firms[] never had its own row in businessEntities() at all (only folded into the taxpayer's total business income) — a real, taxable flow (partner remuneration/interest, and the exempt profit share shown for reconciliation) was invisible in this tab entirely, not just untraceable. A new "Other entities on the return" card now shows any entityGraph entity that has no matching businessEntities row, using the same drill-down UI. Verified live on 3 real profiles: us_resident_indian_income (K-1 ordinary + passive-income edges, and the newly-visible partner-firm card), us_ccorp_indian_sub (the two-root GILTI case — confirmed the entity shows up exactly once on each side, with one real edge between them, not the duplicate-with-self-loop bug Phase 5 found and fixed).
  • ✅ Return-form data threaded into entityGraph, shipped: every entity that previously carried returnForm: null (both roots, India business_entries[]/partner_firms[] graph-only entities, and all three US K-1 kinds) now gets a real form string, reusing the exact wording businessEntitiesResult already used for the K-1/business-entry cases so the flat list and the entity graph can never disagree. New INDIA_KIND_TO_ITR/US_KIND_TO_FORM/US_K1_KIND_TO_FORM maps plus a rootReturnForm() helper in assets-nodes.js (the single-root case defers to the existing "see Filings → Return Form" cross-reference rather than asserting a combined determination it doesn't make). DAG-only — verified via run-assets.js (890/890), the fuzzer (0 new divergences at n=200), a clean npm run audit diff, and tests/engine/run.js unaffected.
  • ✅ Per-entity Filings/Documents, shipped — scoped to what the data actually supports: a new "Per-Entity Return Obligations" card on the Filings tab (EntityReturnFormsCard in Views.jsx) lists every entityGraph entity with its own return form, right below the taxpayer's own consolidated Return Form card — answering "ITR-5 for this firm / 1120-S for this S-corp / etc." directly, without re-scoping the whole DOCUMENTS_CATALOG per entity (most of those documents — FBAR, 8938, treaty forms — are genuinely taxpayer-wide facts, not per-sub-entity ones, so pretending otherwise would have been the same "fake bolt-on" this section warned against). GraphOnlyEntityRow (Business tab) also gained the returnForm display line BusinessEntityRow already had, closing a small pre-existing gap between the two sibling components.
  • ✅ Entity switcher, shipped — deliberately scoped to Business + Filings only, not "every view." EntitySwitcher (a Consolidated (all entities) ▾ dropdown, root entities excluded from the option list since "Consolidated" already is that view) renders in the header utility bar only when view === "business" || view === "filings", with the selection lifted to page.jsx state (selectedEntityId, reset on every client switch to prevent an id collision across two clients' entity graphs — verified live: us_k1_scorp_0 exists on both us_resident_indian_income and us_only_cpa_client with different entities behind it). Selecting an entity filters BusinessView's entity rows and FilingsView's new per-entity card down to that one entity, while explicit on-screen notes say the KPI tiles (Business) and Compliance Calendar/Documents to File (Filings) stay consolidated/unfiltered — the remaining seven tabs (Residency, Reconciliation, Withholding, Holdings, Accounts, Integrations, Structure) were left untouched, per this section's own original caution that most of them mix entity-specific and taxpayer-level facts that don't cleanly reduce to "just entity X's slice."

8. Phased build order

Tracked by Track (India / US / Both) rather than by letter-suffixing one country's work as subordinate to the other's — the previous version of this table numbered India's work "Phase 1" and the equivalent-scope US work "Phase 1b," which is exactly the structural asymmetry this restructure corrects.

Coverage Gap Tracker

Living document tracking everything WISING does not yet model, viewed through three separate lenses — India-only (a taxpayer living solely in India with only Indian income), US-only (solely US), and cross-border — so domestic accuracy is tracked with the same rigor as treaty work.

Last verified against live sources: 11 July 2026. Items marked "verified" were checked by web research on that date, not recalled from memory. Re-verify time-sensitive rows (rates, thresholds, treaty status) every quarter and after every Union Budget / US tax act.

Status legend: ✅ modeled · 🟡 partial · ❌ missing · 🔍 needs code verification · 🚫 out of scope by design

Build Now? legend (added per request — divides every row by whether I can build it without waiting on anything):

  • 🟢 Now — every input already exists somewhere in Layer 1 (India or US); this is engine/UI work only, no form changes needed. Where only part of a row is buildable now, the Detail column says so explicitly.
  • 🟡 Needs field — genuinely blocked until a new Layer 1 field is added (via an Antigravity prompt or direct edit) and round-tripped back.
  • ⚪ N/A — doesn't fit the Now/Needs-field frame: already in flight with a prompt issued, a recorded out-of-scope decision, or needs a Layer 1 coverage check before it can even be classified.

In-product surfacing: every 🚫/assurance row that is not rule-encodable (GAAR, STT, payer-side TDS, clubbing depth, FICA levy, FATCA Ch.4, mocked uploads, plus the MLI/DTAA assurances) is ALSO shown inside the Monitor itself — the "Deliberately out of scope" card at the bottom of the overview, built by buildScopeNotes() in conflicts.js and gated per profile (e.g. the payer-TDS note only appears for business profiles, FICA only when US wages/SE income exist). The tracker records the decision; the Monitor discloses it to the professional.

Priority legend: P1 = real money exposure computable or misstatement risk now · P2 = valuable, needs new Layer 1 fields or moderate build · P3 = completeness/edge audience

A. India-only lens (domestic taxpayer, no US exposure)

IDItemStatusBuild Now?PriorityDetail
IN-1~~Advance-tax / late-filing interest — ss.423/424/425 (old 234A/B/C)~~ FIXED (s.424/425)✅ shipped (partial)——User-directed, after the two single-jurisdiction demo profiles (IN/US-only) exposed how few real compliance-risk findings existed for a domestic-only taxpayer. s.424 (234B, default in paying advance tax): if advance tax paid < 90% of assessed tax (total liability less TDS/TCS), interest = shortfall × 1%/month × months from 1 Apr to the statutory filing due date (4 months non-audit/31 Jul, 7 months audit-case/31 Oct — same s.44AB turnover-threshold test monitoring.js's Compliance Calendar already uses, kept in sync deliberately rather than shared). s.425 (234C, installment deferment): each quarter's own required slice (15/30/30/25% of assessed tax) tested independently against what was actually paid THAT quarter (Layer 1's advance_tax_q1..q4 fields are per-quarter amounts, not cumulative) — 3 months' interest for a Q1-Q3 shortfall, 1 month for Q4, no cross-quarter netting of a later overpayment against an earlier shortfall (matches the real provision). s.423 (234A, late RETURN filing) NOT built — needs the actual return-FILING date, which this single-year-snapshot tool only ever has a statutory DUE date for, never an actual-filed date to measure from; documented as the reason IN-1 stays "partial" rather than fully closing. Known simplification: s.425's narrow income-timing relief (capital gains/lottery/dividends genuinely arising after a quarter's due date) isn't modeled, since Layer 1 doesn't capture per-transaction dates at that granularity — can overstate the estimate for late-year income. Verified: 27-test suite; hand-checked exact (₹1,91,735, matching the formula to the rupee) against india_only_ca_client; sane, real, previously-invisible numbers surfaced across 7 of the 9 pre-existing demo profiles too (₹7,610 to ₹4,14,168) — most had never been tuned for adequate advance-tax coverage since this finding never existed to check it. Refined 16 Jul 2026 (web-verification pass): s.425's presumptive proviso now applied — a business that is ONLY presumptive (s.58, old 44AD/ADA; no regular-books entry, no partner-firm PGBP) owes a single 100% installment by 15 Mar (1 month of interest on a shortfall) instead of the quarterly ladder, and the Compliance Calendar shows that single installment in place of the four quarterly rows. Mixed presumptive+regular profiles conservatively keep the quarterly ladder (the proviso's relief covers "an eligible business", and quarterly is the safe reading for mixed income). Verified against FY2026-27 practitioner guidance (ClearTax/Tax2win/India Briefing); no existing profile's figures changed — the only purely-presumptive demo (Grace Thomas) has zero assessed tax after TDS, checked across all 11. Second refinement, same day (first-class symmetry sweep): the US twin (Form 2210's $1,000 balance-due de minimis) had been built in from the start, but India's own equivalents were missing — now added: the s.404 (old s.208) ₹10,000 floor (no advance-tax obligation at all below it, so ss.424/425 interest cannot arise), and the s.404 senior carve-out (old s.207(2)): a resident individual aged 60+ with no PGBP-head income is exempt from advance tax entirely — common for retired returnees living on pension/interest/CG; "has PGBP" read broadly (any business entry, F&O, speculative, partner-firm) so the exemption never applies to a marginal business case. Verified with 4 synthetic hand-checks (assessed ₹8,700 → silent; resident senior 68 no-PGBP → silent; identical facts age 45 → fires; senior 68 WITH a 44ADA entry → fires, via the presumptive single-installment branch, proving the two same-day refinements compose) plus the 11-profile regression (all figures unchanged).
IN-2Late-filing fee + penalty regime (old 234F ₹5,000; 270A under-reporting 50%/200%)❌🟡 Needs fieldP2Natural companion to IN-1. The fee needs an actual-filing-date field Layer 1 doesn't collect; the 270A under-reporting exposure is disclosure-only and could ship without it.
IN-3Agricultural income — partial integration❌🟡 Needs fieldP2Exempt income that still raises the effective slab rate when agri income > ₹5,000 and total income exceeds the exemption limit. No Layer 1 field exists (only a tooltip mention under misc income). Needs one new field + rate-integration math. Very common for HUF/family profiles.
IN-4Property sale capital gains — engine-side computation🔍🟢 NowP1Layer 1 collects the complete property-sale card (acquisition date/cost, improvements, pre-2001 FMV, transfer expenses, stamp-duty-value override, reinvestment-exemption elections, buyer TDS) but the engine appears to consume only pre-aggregated CG figures — the per-property computation (indexation where applicable, 50C deemed consideration, exemption caps: ₹50L bond limit, ₹10cr cap on residential reinvestment) is not verified engine-side. Verify, then close whichever half is missing.
IN-5AMT for non-corporates (old 115JC, 18.5% on adjusted total income)❌🟡 Needs fieldP3Relevant to firms/LLPs and old-regime individuals claiming specified deductions (10AA/35AD/Ch VI-A part C). Corporate MAT is modeled (book-profit proxy); the non-corporate twin needs the specified-deduction detail Layer 1 doesn't break out. Re-confirmed during Phase 4 (25 Jul 2026), not just re-asserted: checked whether IN-26's own s.35AD field (specified_business_s35AD_inr, wired into business income the same pass) could at least support a conservative, partial ATI test using the one add-back Layer 1 does capture — it can't. That field lives in layer1_india.html's "Special Corporate Business Schemes" section, gated to company entities only (its own helper text: "For Indian Companies only"), and s.115JC (non-corporate AMT) by definition only applies to NON-company entities — companies have their own separate MAT (s.115JB) instead. There is no entity-type overlap between the one s.35AD data point this app collects and the one tax regime that would need it, so even a partial/floor version genuinely has zero real data to compute from. Chapter VI-A Part C (80H-80RRB) and s.10AA (SEZ) — the other two ATI add-back categories — remain uncaptured entirely, as already noted. amt_credit_bf_inr (shared with s.115JD MAT credit) is a plain carryforward-amount input, not ATI derivation detail, and doesn't change this. Stays a genuine "needs field" item, not a build candidate.
IN-6~~Presumptive taxation — s.58 (old 44AD/44ADA/44AE)~~ LOCK-IN DEPTH SHIPPED✅ shipped——Verified current limits: business ₹2cr (₹3cr when cash receipts <5%), professional ₹50L (₹75L when cash <5%), rates 8%/6% and 50% carried into ITA 2025 s.58 unchanged. Layer 1 already has presumptive fields and updateBizPresumptive; turnover-ceiling eligibility tests were already engine-side (see presumptiveEligibilityAgg/usesRegularBooksInr, pre-existing). Phase 4 (25 Jul 2026) closed the remaining depth: layer1_india.html's own validateS44ADEligibility() already force-reverts a UI attempt to re-select s.44AD during the 5-year s.44AD(4) lock-in — but never disclosed the lock-in to the preparer, and never computed s.44AD(5)'s real financial consequence (mandatory tax audit under s.44AB whenever total income exceeds the basic exemption limit in ANY locked-out year, regardless of turnover). New presumptiveLockinAgg node (aggregateindiaincome-nodes.js) parses s44AD_last_exit_ay with the same regex/date math as the live form; a new presumptive_lockin_active_india finding (assets-nodes.js) discloses years remaining and flags mandatory audit when it applies; report-batch1-nodes.js's form_3cb_3cd trigger extended to force the audit document regardless of the usual ₹1cr/₹10cr threshold. DAG-only (docs/DAG_MIGRATION_TRACKER.md §L — the classic engine is frozen). Verified: 9 new hand-checked synthetic cases (run-assets.js) covering active/expired lock-in and above/below the basic exemption limit; 248/248 + 817/817 existing suites unaffected. Given real demo-profile coverage 25 Jul 2026 (docs/DAG_MIGRATION_TRACKER.md §P): india_only_ca_client now carries a real s44AD_last_exit_ay, verified live in the app (Monitor's critical findings list, Filings' Form 3CB/3CD flipping to Required) — monitor-next's own profile list had to be re-pointed from the frozen engine/profiles.js snapshot to the live DAG mirror for this to reach "Switch client…" at all, see §P for the full fix.
IN-7~~Tax-audit threshold flag (s.44AB — ₹1cr / ₹10cr when ≥95% digital)~~ FIXED✅ shipped——Shipped as part of a Filings-tab coverage pass (13 Jul 2026): new form_3cb_3cd document (Form 3CB/3CD, Tax Audit Report), triggered by summing turnover across business_entries[] (same gross-receipts/turnover/digital+cash field-fallback chain as computeBusinessEntryNetProfitInr) against the ₹1cr/₹10cr(≥95%-digital) threshold, OR unconditionally for a company (indiaIsCompany — Companies Act audit is not turnover-gated). The Compliance Calendar's India ITR deadline now also shifts Jul 31 → Oct 31 ("audit case") on the same trigger, instead of always showing the non-audit date regardless of entity type. Verified with synthetic ₹15cr/₹50L/₹105cr turnover fixtures at both sides of the 95%-digital boundary, plus all 9 demo profiles (tests/engine/run.js, 20/20 pass).
IN-8House-property depth: ₹2L loss set-off cap, 30% standard deduction, 1/5 pre-construction amortization🔍🟢 NowP2HP income is aggregated and loss set-off exists, but whether the ₹2L inter-head cap and 1/5 amortization are enforced engine-side needs verification, then closing against data already captured.
IN-9~~Deduction breadth: 80G, 80E, 80DDB, 80U/80DD (old regime)~~ MOSTLY FIXED🟡⚪ N/AP3Layer 1 DOES capture all of these (verified 13 Jul 2026 via a field-coverage audit, see docs/FIELD_COVERAGE_AUDIT.md) — the engine just never read them. Now wired: 80DD/80U (flat ₹75k/₹1.25L by disability severity, matching Layer 1's own "standard"/"severe" resolution), 80DDB (actual expense capped ₹40k/₹1L by Layer 1's own "normal"/"senior" patient category), 80E (uncapped education-loan interest), 80EEA/80EE (capped by sanction-date window — ₹50k for FY16-17-sanctioned, ₹1.5L for FY19-22-sanctioned, 0 outside both), 80GGB/GGC (uncapped political donations), 80GG (rent-paid-no-HRA, the classic 3-way minimum against gross total income, applied in computeIndiaTax where GTI is available). Still open: 80G (donations) — per-entry 50%/100% with/without-qualifying-limit categorization needs gross-total-income-dependent capping logic per donation category, deliberately not guessed at; 80M (inter-corporate dividend) — business-entity-only, low relevance for the individual-focused profiles.
IN-10s.89 salary-arrears relief (Form 10E)❌🟡 Needs fieldP3Needs new fields (arrears by year). Niche but real for job-switchers/PSU arrears.
IN-11Clubbing depth (s.64 old)🟡⚪ N/AP3Raw clubbed amounts are captured and taxed; no detection/validation logic (e.g. spouse-asset-transfer tracing). Accepted as data-entry-driven — recorded decision, not a build item.
IN-12s.194P (75+ senior, bank files for them — no return needed)❌🟢 NowP3Trivial disclosure from DOB + income mix.
IN-13STT (raised on F&O by Finance Act 2026)🚫⚪ N/A—Transaction tax, not income tax. stt_paid flags already drive the CG regime correctly; the levy itself stays out of scope.
IN-14GST🚫⚪ N/A—Indirect tax; out of scope by design. Note only so the decision is recorded.

IN-36 · Old-vs-new regime comparison — the engine computes ONLY the regime already chosen on Layer 1 (model.residency.india.taxRegime, default NEW); no side-by-side, no "you chose the costlier regime" finding · ❌ · 🟢 Now · P1 · Found during the 16 Jul 2026 "what does a tax pro actually buy" audit. "Which regime should my client pick" is the single most common question an Indian CA answers for every individual client every year, and every mainstream India prep suite ships a comparator — its absence is conspicuous in a CA-facing demo. All inputs already exist: computeIndiaTax is already fully parameterized by regime (slabs, §156/87A caps, and the deduction set all branch on isNew), so running it twice and surfacing the delta plus a regime_suboptimal finding when the chosen regime is costlier is pure engine/UI work, zero new fields. One real subtlety to model honestly: s.115BAC(6) — a taxpayer WITH business/professional income can switch out of the new regime only once in a lifetime (Form 10-IEA) while the salaried can flip annually, so the finding must frame "old is cheaper this year" much more cautiously for business filers (this year's saving can cost more later).

IN-39 · ~~DAG-only: the DAG's capitalGainsComputation gates its ENTIRE financial_holdings.transactions read behind isIndiaRor (one shared array feeds both the foreign-holdings loop and the listed_equity/mutual-fund/bond/etc. loop) — unlike the real engine, which only gates the foreign-holdings-specific read and deliberately leaves India-registered instruments ungated (source-rule income, taxable regardless of residency). US-1~~Estimated-tax underpayment penalty (§6654 / Form 2210)~~ FIXED✅ shipped——A partial version of this finding already existed (90%-of-current-year safe harbor + raw balance-due as a stand-in amount, explicitly self-documented as not checking the prior-year harbor) — found while building IN-1's mirror. Real gap: Layer 1 US's #with-prior-tax input (withholding_and_estimated.prior_year_total_tax_usd) was collected but never read anywhere in the engine, so the 100%/110%-of-prior-year safe harbor could never actually be checked, only asserted as "not checked" in the finding's own text. Now wired (null, not 0, when never entered, so "no prior-year tax" isn't confused with "not answered"); the 110% multiplier (vs 100%) applies when current-year AGI exceeds $150k, used as a practical proxy for prior-year AGI since Layer 1 has no such field. Also upgraded from showing the raw balance-due figure to an actual estimated PENALTY dollar amount: simplified Form 2210 Part III regular method — four equal required installments, withholding spread evenly across all four quarters (the real IRS convention), estimated payments applied to their own quarter, each quarter's shortfall accruing independently (no cross-quarter netting) at that quarter's verified TY2026 IRS underpayment rate (7%/6%/7% Q1-Q3; Q4 approximated at Q3's rate, documented as unverified) for the months remaining to the following 15 Apr. US-30 · FTC carryovers exist only as an OUTPUT — no prior-year carryover input field, no per-basket §904 limitation, no 1-back/10-forward expiry tracking (Form 1116 Schedule B) · ❌ · 🟢 Now (current-year basket split) / 🟡 Needs field (prior-year carryover input) · P1 · Found during the 16 Jul 2026 "what does a tax pro actually buy" audit, verified by grep: computeFtc computes THIS year's excess Indian tax as usCarryover and reports it, but nothing ever accepts LAST year's unused credit back in — and the passive/general basket distinction appears only in human-readable trace prose ("Form 1116 general basket", "Passive basket"), never in the arithmetic: the §904 limitation is computed on one aggregate pool, so passive-heavy Indian income (interest/dividends/CG) can wrongly absorb limitation headroom created by general-basket wages and vice versa. Practitioner sources (IRS i1116; multiple expat-practice guides, checked 16 Jul 2026) confirm basket-locked carryovers (excess in one basket can never offset the other) plus Schedule B carryover reconciliation are among the most common Form 1116 errors. Current-year per-basket split is buildable now — the model already classifies every income stream by type; the carryover input needs a Layer 1 field (per-basket prior-year unused credit). Single-year-snapshot caveat: like India's carryForwardLosses, this would be an entered opening balance, not true multi-year tracking.

US-31 · No filing-status / election comparators — the US twins of IN-36: MFJ-vs-MFS is never computed side-by-side (including the §6013(g) full-year-election variant for an NRA spouse, THE arrival-year decision for the corridor), and FEIE-vs-FTC-only is gated for eligibility but never compared for outcome · ❌ · 🟢 Now · P2 · Found in the 16 Jul 2026 first-class symmetry sweep, prompted by asking what the US-side equivalent of IN-36 (regime comparison) is. The engine already handles MFS within a chosen status correctly everywhere it matters (SALT $20k/$250k MFS halving, tips/overtime denial, AMT break halving — all verified present), and FEIE has real eligibility gating plus the §911 no-double-dip — but neither choice is ever computed BOTH ways with a delta shown, even though computeUsTax is fully parameterized by filing status (same run-it-twice shape as IN-36) and the FEIE-vs-FTC choice is a genuine annual computation decision for every US-citizen-in-India profile (revoking FEIE carries a 5-year re-election bar — §911(e)(2) — so the comparator must carry that caveat, the mirror of IN-36's once-in-a-lifetime 10-IEA caution). Both are "show both sides of the auto-decision" (CL-5) instances with real dollar deltas.

US-32 · State-level PTE (pass-through entity) elective tax on S-corp/partnership K-1s — entity-level SALT-cap workaround, entirely unmodeled · ❌ · 🟡 Needs field · P2 · Raised 25 Jul 2026 while explaining why an S-corp files its own return without paying its own federal tax: several states (CA's AB 150 PTET, NY's PTET, NJ's BAIT, 30+ others post-TCJA) let a partnership/S-corp elect to pay state tax AT THE ENTITY level specifically so its owners can route around the $10k federal SALT cap — a real, current, and for pass-through owners in high-tax states, financially significant mechanism this app doesn't touch at all today. Confirmed via direct grep: no K-1 entity in Layer 1 US carries a "state" field, let alone a PTE-election flag or qualified-income base — businessEntitiesResult/buildEntityGraph's K-1 handling (assets-nodes.js) only ever produces filesOwnReturn: false, returnForm: "...informational..." for every K-1 type, with no entity-level tax computation at all, federal or state. A correct model needs FOUR connected pieces, not one, or it actively misleads rather than just under-informs: (1) new Layer 1 field(s) on each K-1 entity — PTE election yes/no, which state, qualified net income base; (2) state-specific rate formulas — CA flat 9.3%, NY's own graduated PTET brackets, NJ's BAIT schedule (scope to these 3 first, matching the only 3 states this app's OWN individual state tax — US-12/H.7.1 — already models; treating the other 25+ PTE states as a documented, deliberate gap rather than guessed at); (3) a feed-back reducing the K-1's OWN federal ordinary income by its allocable share of PTE tax paid (the entity deducts it as a business expense before the K-1 reaches the owner — this is the entire mechanism the SALT-cap workaround relies on, so omitting it would show a net tax INCREASE where the real effect is closer to neutral); (4) an offsetting credit on the OWNER's own state return for their share of PTE tax the entity already paid (skip this and the model shows the entity's tax with no relief on the individual side, which is straightforwardly wrong, not just incomplete). Sibling to US-12 (individual state tax) but a separate, entity-level build — not a sub-item of it. Not started; no code written.

Buildable-now count (US): 9 of 20 remaining (3 partial) (US-1, US-2, US-5, US-16, US-17, US-19, US-20, US-21, US-26, US-27, US-28, US-29 shipped) — US-3 (partial), US-7, US-9, US-12, US-13, US-14, US-18 (partial), US-30 (partial — basket split now, carryover input needs field), US-31, US-32 (needs field).

XB-18~~Monitor's "Residency Day-Counters" panel showed a day-count presence-test bar for EVERY taxpayer, including companies/HUFs/firms/US entity-type filers — none of which have a day-count residency test at all~~ FIXED✅ shipped——User-directed, spotted from a live screenshot of a foreign-holdco profile showing a fabricated "365/182 days" bar. XB-19 · ~~The Monitor showed a fabricated DUAL-jurisdiction picture (both country badges, both residency Flag cards, a DTAA Treaty Position card) for taxpayers with zero real presence on one side~~ FIXED · ✅ shipped · — · — · User-directed, found immediately after XB-18 shipped: the header badge for Meridian Holdings (a Singapore company with POEM in India, zero US days/citizenship/green-card/US-source income) read "🇮🇳 Company / 🇺🇸 Individual"; a first pass mirrored India's label to get "🇮🇳 Company / 🇺🇸 Company", but the user correctly rejected that too — Meridian has no company (or anything else) in the US at all, and "how can US be an HUF?" for Sharma HUF made the same point even sharper. Root cause: Layer 1's india/us bundle always exists as a shell so neither form crashes if opened, and Router's own jurisdiction toggle (a real "Dual"/"India only"/"US only" selector on router.html) defaults to "dual" and was never overridden on any of the 9 demo profiles — so model.meta.jurisdiction read "dual" even for taxpayers with nothing on one side. Fixed by computing scope dynamically instead of trusting either signal: new model.meta.hasIndiaScope/hasUsScope derive from real reported facts (router.us_days > 0, is_us_citizen, has_green_card, has_us_source_income_or_assets) — Router's own explicit "India only"/"US only" choice still wins outright when a preparer deliberately sets it, but the default "dual" state is now checked against what was actually reported rather than trusted blindly. model.meta.jurisdiction itself is recomputed to this effective value so existing consumers (taxComputation/summary) stay correct too. The Header badge row, the two Residency Flag cards, the Residency Determination panel's entries, and the DTAA Treaty Position card (Article 4 tie-breaker / TRC / Form 10F / PE / 1040-NR are all meaningless without real dual exposure) now render only the in-scope country(ies) for a single-jurisdiction taxpayer, while the Residency & Treaty Conflicts panel stays (still surfaces genuine single-country findings). Verified: 25-test suite; live screenshots — Meridian and Sharma HUF now show India-only end-to-end (one header badge, one Flag card, one Residency Determination entry, no DTAA card, period string reads "TY2026-27 (India)" only); Cloudspire Inc (us_ccorp_indian_sub — a genuinely distinct US C-corp + Indian subsidiary, not a scope gap) and every dual individual profile (e.g. dual_resident_h1b) unchanged, still show both sides in full.

XB-20 · Foreign retirement-account accrual/withdrawal timing mismatch — India's Section 158 (ITA 2025, successor to s.89A/Finance Act 2021) relief election, Form 40 (replaces Form 10EE) · ❌ · 🟡 Needs field · P1 · Verified via web research 16 Jul 2026 (not previously tracked anywhere in this doc). India taxes on accrual; the US taxes 401(k)/IRA growth only on withdrawal — absent an election, a returning NRI's retirement account is taxed in India every year on unrealized growth it hasn't touched, with no matching US tax paid that year to credit against it (FTC needs same-year, same-income tax paid in both countries — a structural mismatch, not something FTC alone fixes). Section 158 lets a "specified person" (India-resident who opened the account while NR-in-India/resident-in-the-notified-country) elect to defer Indian taxation on a "specified account" until the US itself taxes it (withdrawal/redemption) — filed via Form 40 by the return due date. Separately, DTAA Article 20 (private pensions) taxes periodic 401(k)/pension payments only in the country of residence, but Article 23 ("Other Income") governs lump-sum withdrawals, which both countries can tax — a real, taxpayer-facing classification decision this app doesn't currently model at all. Layer 1 US collects 401(k)/IRA/pension contributions in detail (retirement_accounts.) but distributions* (ira_distributions_usd, 401k_distributions_usd) have no UI at all (see US-24) — and neither side has a field for the Section 158 election itself, the account's "specified account" status, or periodic-vs-lump-sum characterization. A real RNOR-aware disclosure finding (flag the exposure once RNOR ends and distributions exist, point to Form 40/Section 158 and the Article 20/23 distinction) is buildable once US-24 wires the distribution fields; the election/characterization fields themselves are a genuine Layer 1 build, not just an engine fix.

XB-21 · FAST-DS 2026 (Foreign Assets of Small Taxpayers – Disclosure Scheme) — a one-time 6-month amnesty window (commencement date not yet notified) for undisclosed foreign assets/income, relevant to every taxpayer already flagged by XB-7's Black Money Act exposure · ❌ · 🟢 Now (disclosure only) · P2 · Verified via web research 16 Jul 2026, Budget 2026 (Finance Bill 2026, Clauses 114-128). Two tracks: Category A (asset/income never taxed or reported) pays 60% (30% tax + 30% penalty) of value as of 31 Mar 2026; Category B (asset bought from already-taxed income, or acquired while genuinely NRI) pays a flat ₹1,00,000 fee — both buy immunity from further demand/penalty/prosecution under the Black Money Act. A supplementary immunity also exists for non-immovable foreign assets under ₹20L aggregate, independent of the scheme. Buildable now as a qualitative disclosure addendum to the existing XB-7 finding (same trigger facts — Schedule FA-relevant foreign holdings), not a new data requirement; commencement date being unnotified means don't hardcode a filing deadline yet.

XB-22 · OBBBA 1% federal excise tax on cash-funded remittances abroad (effective 1 Jan 2026) · ❌ · 🟡 Needs field · P3 · Verified via web research 16 Jul 2026. A new US-side sender tax — 1% of the transfer amount, collected by the remittance provider — but applies ONLY to transfers funded by physical cash, money order, or cashier's check; transfers funded from a US bank account, debit card, or credit card are exempt. Real-world hit rate for this app's typical NRI-to-India wire/ACH transfers is likely low (most flow through bank-funded channels, not cash), but it's a genuine new 2026 US tax with zero current field to capture "was this outbound transfer cash-funded" — distinct from India's own LRS outbound-remittance tracking (lrs_outbound, which governs money leaving INDIA, not money leaving the US). Lower priority than XB-20/XB-21 given the narrow cash-funded scope, but flagged so it isn't missed if a real cash-remittance profile shows up.

XB-23 · ~~Several cross-border findings (FTC shortfall, Form 44, FBAR, tax-year apportionment, FX-basis, 1099-DA awareness) and the world-map jurisdiction count fired regardless of whether the taxpayer actually had exposure in the second country~~ FIXED · ✅ shipped · — · — · Found immediately after building the india_only_ca_client/us_only_cpa_client demo profiles (single-jurisdiction, no US/India exposure at all): a purely domestic Indian resident was shown a CRITICAL "Foreign Tax Credit shortfall — residual double taxation" (there is no double taxation when only one country ever taxed her), a CRITICAL "FBAR threshold breached" (she is not a US person at all), and an INFO "Form 44 required" / "check for US Form 1099-DA" / "Tax-year apportionment" / "FX conversion basis" — none applicable to a single-country taxpayer. Root causes, both distinct from XB-19 (which only fixed the Monitor's residency-panel DISPLAY, not the underlying finding-generation logic): (1) conflicts.js's finding gates used the structural model.meta.hasIndia/hasUs (true whenever Layer 1's shell object exists, i.e. always) instead of the data-driven hasIndiaScope/hasUsScope (XB-19) — fixed across 9 call sites (conflicts.js FORM 2210, 1099-DA awareness, FTC gap/available, Form 44, tax-year apportionment, FX basis, form_26as_ais_tis/form_16_16a/form_4868 document flags, buildScopeNotes's dual gate; computation.js's indiaItrForm solver gate); (2) aggregateAccounts (normalize.js) unconditionally summed a taxpayer's own bank accounts into the "FBAR aggregate peak" whenever Layer 1 US never separately computed one — correct for a genuine dual taxpayer (their India accounts ARE foreign-to-them-as-a-US-person) but wrong for a taxpayer with no US nexus at all, whose own domestic accounts aren't "foreign" to anyone; now takes a hasUsScope parameter and returns $0 when false; (3) countriesFromEngine (lib/wising.js) always returned both India AND US country objects, so an out-of-scope country rendered as a green "On track" tracked jurisdiction (and counted toward "ALL JURISDICTIONS") instead of the map's own pre-existing "Not tracked" gray default for any country with no status entry — now omits an out-of-scope country entirely, letting it fall through to that existing treatment for free. Verified: 27-test suite (up from 25 — the 2 new demo profiles feed the same smoke-test loop automatically); both new profiles hand-checked to show zero cross-border findings and a correct 1-jurisdiction map; all 6 existing dual-jurisdiction profiles hand-checked to show their FTC/Form-44/apportionment/FX/FBAR findings unchanged (no regression).

XB-24 · ~~The "FTC Reconciliation" card showed a nonzero "Net Unrelieved Double Tax" for a taxpayer with zero exposure in the second country — the same class of bug as XB-23, in a third, separate location it didn't reach~~ FIXED · ✅ shipped · — · — · Found immediately after XB-23 shipped, while visually re-checking the Reconciliation tab (not just the Conflicts panel) for india_only_ca_client: the card showed $48,987 in red "Net Unrelieved Double Tax" for a taxpayer with zero US exposure at all — the exact wrong claim XB-23 had just fixed in the Conflicts-panel finding, but this card is fed by computeFtc() (computation.js) directly, a genuinely separate code path from the detectConflicts() finding XB-23 gated. Root cause: computeFtc() only zeroed the US-direction credit computation for usTax.isNra (Form 1040-NR filers) — a narrower condition than "has no US exposure at all"; a taxpayer who isn't a US taxpayer in ANY sense (not even NRA, since no US return is filed at all) fell through to the "normal dual taxpayer" branch, which unconditionally treated 100% of her India income as "foreign-source relative to a US return." Fixed by extending the SAME zero-out the code already used for NRA to also cover !model.meta.hasUsScope, in two places — the initial foreignSrcGrossUsd assignment, and (caught on the first verification pass, not guessed) the separate creditableFraction fallback a few lines below, whose own code comment already explicitly warned this exact : 1 fallback would "wrongly multiply a real India tax figure by 1" if the numerator were zeroed without also gating the fallback — fixing only the first line reproduced precisely the bug that comment described. Verified: 27-test suite; computed.ftc.netUnrelievedDoubleTaxUsd confirmed $0 for both india_only_ca_client and us_only_cpa_client (previously $48,987/already-correct-$0 respectively), unchanged (still nonzero, real figures) for all dual-jurisdiction profiles; live screenshot of the Reconciliation tab.

XB-25 · Dual-status year (the move year itself) not modeled — an arrival/departure-year taxpayer is computed as a full-year single-status filer; Layer 1's own dual-status fields are collected but never read · ❌ · 🟢 Now (disclosure/eligibility half) / 🟡 (split-year computation) · P1 · Found during a user-prompted "is cross-border coverage really enough?" audit (16 Jul 2026). The move year is arguably the single most common cross-border engagement — every new H-1B arrival and every returning NRI has exactly one — and it's the one scenario the engine can't represent: the US residency solver only ever produces a full-year status, and constants.js defines RESIDENCY.DUAL_STATUS that NOTHING ever assigns (dead constant since it was written). Meanwhile layer1_us.html already collects the key facts — dual_status_arrival_date (#res-dual-arrival), first_year_choice_entry_date/first_year_choice_election (§7701(b)(4) First-Year Choice), and s6013g_joint_election (full-year-resident MFJ election for a dual-status spouse) — and normalize.js reads NONE of them (verified by grep: the only §6013 field the engine reads is the separate nra_specific.s6013h_joint_election); same "collected but never read" class as IN-28/FIELD_COVERAGE_AUDIT. Real consequences of dual-status the engine currently gets silently wrong for a move-year filer: no standard deduction (dual-status filers must itemize — the engine hands them the full standard deduction today), no MFJ / no joint standard deduction absent a §6013(g)/(h) election, nonresident-period income taxed only if US-source (1040-NR statement) vs resident-period worldwide — and the §6013(g) full-year-MFJ election is a genuine computation trade-off (joint rates + standard deduction vs worldwide taxation of the pre-arrival period) a corridor pro decides every arrival year. India side needs NO change (s.6 status genuinely applies to the whole FY — no split-year concept in Indian domestic law; the existing FY/CY apportionment already handles the calendar mismatch). Buildable NOW from existing fields: a disclosure finding (dual-status filing consequences + First-Year-Choice/§6013(g) election framing) and the standard-deduction/MFJ eligibility gates. Needs more: the actual split-year computation requires income split at the residency-start date, which Layer 1 only captures annually (the India side's quarterly structure doesn't cover US-source income).

XB-26 · Roth accounts — Layer 1 US collects Roth IRA/401(k) contributions and a backdoor-Roth flag; the engine reads NONE of it, and the India side of the trap (India does not recognize Roth's tax-free character) is unmodeled · ❌ · 🟢 Now (disclosure) / 🟡 Needs field (computation) · P2 · Found during the 16 Jul 2026 "what does a tax pro actually buy" audit, verified by grep: roth_ira_contribution_usd, roth_401k_contribution_usd, backdoor_roth_executed exist only in layer1_us.html — zero engine references (same "collected but never read" class as IN-28/XB-25). Two distinct real issues: (1) US-side — a backdoor Roth without checking the §408(d)(2) pro-rata rule (existing pre-tax IRA balances make part of the conversion taxable, Form 8606) is a classic self-inflicted wound, and the direct-contribution MAGI phase-out is checkable from AGI already in the model; (2) India-side — for a returning NRI who becomes ROR, India taxes Roth earnings as ordinary investment income (no Indian recognition of the US tax-free wrapper; and s.158/Form 40 relief — XB-20 — is framed around tax-DEFERRED accounts whose US tax comes at withdrawal, a poor fit for Roth where US tax already happened at contribution — making Roth arguably WORSE than a 401(k) to carry back to India, the opposite of its US-side reputation). A disclosure finding for both is buildable from existing fields; quantification needs balances/distributions (the same missing fields as US-24/XB-20).

XB-27 · NRE vs NRO account-type distinction absent — the most-cited practitioner NRI trap (NRE FD interest exempt in India but fully taxable in the US) cannot even be represented in the model · ❌ · 🟡 Needs field · P2 · Same audit. Practitioner checklists (checked 16 Jul 2026) consistently rank "NRE interest is tax-free in India but must appear on the 1040 as interest income" among the top mistakes CPAs catch in NRI returns, alongside its mirror (NRO interest carries 30%-plus-surcharge TDS u/s 195 that the US return should be claiming as FTC). Layer 1 India's interest fields (interest_savings_inr, interest_fd_rd_inr) carry no NRE/NRO/resident account-type tag — the only NRE/NRO structures that exist anywhere are nro_repatriation (itself unread, IN-30) and an s.197 lower-TDS-certificate NRO_INTEREST checkbox (also unread, IN-16) — so the engine cannot (a) exempt NRE interest India-side for an NR/RNOR, (b) flag it as US-taxable-anyway for a US person, or (c) apply the NRO TDS expectation to the withholding reconciliation. Needs a per-account or per-stream account-type field; once tagged, all three consequences are engine-ready (the US-side worldwide-interest path already exists).

XB-28 · ~~The Compliance Calendar and the Limit Monitoring gauges showed BOTH jurisdictions' entries regardless of scope — a US-only client saw India advance-tax quarters and ITR deadlines; an India-only client (including all three India-only entity profiles) saw US estimated-tax dates, Form 1040 deadlines, and $0 FBAR/Form 8938 gauges~~ FIXED · ✅ shipped · — · — · The scope-blindness class (XB-19 display / XB-23 findings+FBAR+map / XB-24 computeFtc) in a FOURTH and FIFTH location, found 16 Jul 2026 while verifying the s.425 presumptive-proviso fix across profiles: monitoring.js's deadline builder had no scope check at all (and the UI renders calendar.all unfiltered), and computation.js's computeLimits built the FBAR/8938 gauges unconditionally (an India-only taxpayer seeing an FBAR gauge — even at $0 — reads as "this tool thinks I'm a US filer", a guaranteed known-answer-test failure for a CA) and the LRS/NRO gauges unconditionally (mirror problem for a US-only client). Fixed with the same model.meta.hasIndiaScope/hasUsScope gates as the rest of the class: calendar rows filtered by jur, FBAR/8938 gauges gated on US scope, LRS/NRO on India scope (FEIE/§530A gauges already self-gate on real US-side facts). Verified: 27-test suite; all 11 profiles checked — Kavya/David/Nimbus/Meridian/Sharma HUF now show only their own jurisdiction's deadlines and gauges, Grace Thomas additionally shows the single presumptive advance-tax installment, every dual profile unchanged (6 IN + 6 US deadlines, full gauge set).

Buildable-now count (cross-border): 4 of 20 remaining (3 partial) — XB-3 (partial), XB-21, XB-25 (partial — disclosure/eligibility half now, split-year computation needs per-period income), XB-26 (partial — disclosure now, computation needs balance/distribution fields). Both stay in the 🟡 bucket below; corrected count was 6, is now 4.

F. Confirmatory (green-state) layer — recorded 16 Jul 2026

Distinct from every gap above: these aren't missing detections, they're missing confirmations. A pro evaluating the product first runs known-answer tests — clients whose facts they already understand — and checks whether the product shows what they'd have concluded themselves; only after that trust is earned do the novel findings (the conflicts they DIDN'T know) carry weight. Today the engine evaluates dozens of gates silently and speaks only on failure, so "checked and correctly not required" is indistinguishable from "never checked" — the worst possible answer to a known-answer test. What already serves this layer well (verified 16 Jul 2026): both countries' full computation waterfalls with click-to-expand traces, the loss-set-off table, withholding expected-vs-actual rows, residency/ITR-form/FTC determinations with facts, six limit gauges (FBAR/8938/LRS/NRO-repatriation/FEIE/§530A) with pace projections, form-aware compliance calendar, and the "deliberately out of scope" honesty card.

CL-1~~Silent passes — a "checks run" registry~~ SHIPPED, DAG-ONLY✅ shipped—Built 20 Jul 2026, explicitly DAG-only per direct instruction ("make sure that we make all the relevant changes only to DAG and not to the Engine") — prototypes/graph-pilot/checks-registry-nodes.js is a brand-new node, engine/*.js untouched. Covers ~40 of the ~60 finding gates (the ones with a real, meaningful "evaluated and came back clean" state — see the file's own header for the ones deliberately excluded: informational findings that always show whenever their topic is relevant have no missing negative-space signal to fill in). Reads only already-computed values every findings-batchN node already reads — zero new raw leaves, and it never touches, wraps, or modifies a single existing findings node, so there's no risk of drifting the ~60 already-fuzz-verified finding conditions. Rendered as a collapsed "✓ N checks passed" panel under the Conflicts list (ChecksRegistryPanel, monitor-next/components/Views.jsx), both on the main Monitor overview and filtered to residency/treaty on the Residency tab. Verified: run-checksregistry.js (37/37 — zero fired/passed ID overlap across all 12 real fixtures, nonzero passed count on every one) plus a new fuzz-harness invariant (run-fuzz.js now asserts zero fired/passed overlap on every one of thousands of randomly mutated profiles — 3000+5000 iterations, two fresh seeds, 0 violations). Because there's no engine equivalent, this can't be differential-tested against production the way everything else in this migration has been — see DAG_MIGRATION_TRACKER.md's own CL-1 entry for the accepted asymmetry (Engine mode in monitor-next never shows this panel; only DAG mode does).

Reusing the same math prospectively gives "Q3 installment due 15 Dec: ₹4.8L to stay penalty-free" and the 1040-ES/safe-harbor equivalent. Contribution figures are already collected on both Layer 1 forms; caps are already in constants.js (deduction caps) or trivially added (US elective limits).
CL-5Show both sides of every auto-decision🟢 NowP2The US computation auto-picks max(standard, itemized) but displays only the winner; show the loser's figure and the delta ("itemized $31,400 vs standard $30,000 — itemizing saves ~$336"). IN-36 (regime comparison) is the same class on the India side and the highest-value instance. A pro checking the product's judgment needs to see that the alternative was computed, not just that an answer came out.

G. Practice-deliverable layer — recorded 16 Jul 2026

Distinct from sections A-F: not a detection gap and not a confirmation gap — a missing OUTPUT FORMAT for what the engine already computes. Prompted by a direct product question ("will tax pros actually pay for this, even single-jurisdiction?"): today every finding, waterfall, and reconciliation renders only inside the Monitor, which the pro looks at — the client never sees anything, so the product has no artifact that starts a billable advisory conversation. Explicitly scoped OUT of this: tax PLANNING (recommendations, strategy, "you should elect X") — deliberately deferred by product decision, not a gap, since an eventual AI layer is expected to do that better than hardcoded rules, and hardcoded planning logic ages badly against every Finance Act / OBBBA-style act anyway. This section is reporting only: a templated, client-facing rendering of figures the engine has already computed and verified, the same "state the current position" register as a lab report, not a treatment plan.
