A templated PDF/print view assembling existing findings/computed/monitoring output under the firm's own branding is pure rendering — zero new tax logic, same category of work as the CL-section items. Market-validated as a real differentiator (2026 tax-planning-software research, not just an internal guess): "a professionally formatted plan with executive summary and roadmap justifies advisory pricing" and "the best platforms produce branded PDF reports CPAs deliver directly to clients" are cited as concrete purchase drivers, not planning-software fluff. Deliberately the reporting layer, not planning — see section intro. Sequencing note: naturally consumes CL-1's checks-run registry once built (a client report that shows only failures reads as alarmist; one that also shows what was checked and passed reads as thorough). Before this ships as literal prefilled-return content (not just an advisory report), read section H below — the bar for what "good enough" means changes materially.

H. Prefilled-return / e-filing readiness — recorded 21 Jul 2026

The product direction is now explicit: prefilled, downloadable tax returns and forms (users have already entered the relevant facts on Layer 1) come before PD-1's branded advisory report matures further, with e-filing to follow. That changes the acceptable-error bar for anything that reaches documents/taxComputation/returnForms/ftcReport/withholding — those fields stop being an advisory finding with a "confirm with a preparer" recommendation attached, and start being literal content on a form a taxpayer might submit as-is. Everything in sections A-G above was scoped and prioritized against the advisory bar; this section re-examines the same engine against the filing bar, prompted directly by that product clarification rather than a code review.

H.1 — Verified invariant: Monitor's pace-projected fields don't currently leak into filing output. monitoring.js carries two families of "~"/"at current pace" projected dates — the residency day-counter's crossDate/flipDate/dateLabel (line ~79-90, an average-pace extrapolation from an aggregate day-count, not a real arrival-date reconstruction — see the Monitor's own residency-date discussion) and the limit gauges' breachDate/dateLabel (line ~226-228, same linear methodology applied to FBAR/8938/LRS/FEIE). Grepped conflicts.js's six buildXxx functions that assemble documents/taxComputation/returnForms/ftcReport/withholding/scopeNotes (buildDocuments, buildFtcReport, buildTaxComputation, buildWithholdingSummary, buildScopeNotes, buildReturnFormDetermination) — none of them reference monitoring.*, dateLabel, crossDate, flipDate, or breachDate anywhere. The only monitoring field reaching summary is monitoring.calendar.next.dateLabel, which is the Compliance Calendar's own label — computed from real fixed statutory due dates (literal d(year, month, day) calls), not a pace projection; a different, reliable computation that happens to share a field name with the risky ones. Rule to preserve, not just a one-time finding: any future monitoring.js field carrying a projected/"~" value must never be read by those six buildXxx functions or by any prefill/PDF/e-file exporter built on top of them. If a real filing artifact ever needs an actual residency-start date (US dual-status returns have one), that requires Layer 1 to collect a real arrival date — not a better version of the pace formula, since no formula can produce information that was never entered.

H.2 — What already exists to build on. computed.reconciliation's cross-basis rows already carry an estimate: true field (computation.js:1561, the US-business-income-approximated-at-Indian-net row) that the UI surfaces as a small amber "est." badge (monitor-next/components/Views.jsx:800). That's the right pattern — a machine-readable provenance flag the render layer turns into a visible caveat — just applied in exactly one place out of the roughly fifteen that would need it once the same data starts becoming form content instead of a dashboard number.

H.3 — The actual list: self-documented simplifications that feed documents/taxComputation/returnForms/ftcReport/withholding today (i.e., would land on a prefilled form as-is, not just inside a finding's advisory text). Every item below is quoted from the engine's own existing comments, not newly discovered — the gap is that none of them currently carry a machine-readable flag the way H.2's one example does.

Completeness gaps — a required form/schedule/line could be silently absent, which is worse than an approximate number:

  • CFC/GILTI (normalize.js:2698-2700): returnForm: "Foreign local return (not modeled) + Form 5471 (informational, US) + GILTI on Schedule 1" — the foreign local return isn't identified at all for a taxpayer with a CFC, and the GILTI income figure itself is "a hand-entered estimate, since full GILTI/QBAI/tested-income computation from the CFC's own books isn't modeled yet." Prefilling for a CFC-holding taxpayer today would omit a required filing and present an unverified number as if it were computed.
  • Speculative-loss carry-forward (conflicts.js:985, 999): "not modeled at all" in the loss set-off table that feeds Schedule CFL/BFLA — a real carry-forward bucket the schedule would simply omit for a taxpayer who has one.

Numeric approximations feeding a real return-line amount:

  • India surcharge marginal relief (computation.js:452, 740): "simplified marginal relief" — feeds the actual India tax liability line.
  • DTAA NR-interest marginal rate (computation.js:603-606): "a planning-grade approximation that doesn't account for loss-set-off ordering" — feeds the s.115A/s.159 income-tax computation.
  • US business income (computation.js:1561-1563): "US net approximated at Indian net — diverges with US depreciation schedule" — feeds actual US Schedule C net income; already carries H.2's estimate: true flag in the reconciliation view, but that flag doesn't propagate into taxComputation itself.
  • India ss.424/425 interest (conflicts.js:415-417) + US Form 2210 penalty (conflicts.js:471-521): both are literal return lines (ITR Part B-TTI's interest schedule; Form 2210 itself), not just advisory findings. s.425's narrow income-timing relief "isn't modeled... can overstate the estimate for late-year income"; Form 2210's Q4 rate is "approximated at Q3's rate — not separately verified."
  • §72(t) early-withdrawal penalty (conflicts.js:531-556): assumes the full 10% whenever the age test fails, with zero statutory exceptions modeled (death, disability, SEPP, first-time-homebuyer, education, medical, birth/adoption) — a real Form 5329 line that could be materially wrong for anyone who genuinely qualifies for an exception.
  • FTC average-rate approximation (conflicts.js:921-932): "the general FTC computation only approximates" the real graduated-rate mismatch — a real Form 1116 credit amount.
  • FX conversion basis (conflicts.js:1103): stated plainly as "an approximation" — foundational, touches every cross-border dollar figure on both returns.
  • Schedule FA / Black Money Act asset values (conflicts.js:1363): "approximated" — arguably the highest-stakes item on this list; Schedule FA is a heavily-scrutinized disclosure and Black Money Act misreporting carries severe penalty exposure, so an approximated value here is a materially different risk class than an approximated interest estimate.

H.4 — Recommendation, two tracks, not one:

  1. Before any of the above ships as prefilled content, extend H.2's estimate/provenance pattern to documents/taxComputation/returnForms/ftcReport/withholding, and have the download/PDF template (PD-1) render an explicit "Estimated — confirm before filing" marker on every such line — not a plain number indistinguishable from a verified one. This is buildable immediately, independent of fixing any underlying simplification, and is the single highest-leverage thing to do before a prefilled form reaches a user.
  2. Prioritize closing gaps by real-world stakes, not code complexity. Completeness gaps (CFC/foreign-return-not-modeled, speculative-loss-not-modeled) and high-penalty-exposure items (Schedule FA/Black Money Act, GILTI) should be closed or explicitly gated — "this profile has a fact pattern this platform doesn't yet support for prefill; consult a preparer" — before numeric refinements (interest-rate approximations, marginal-relief simplifications) that are directionally correct and merely imprecise.

H.5 — Entity-agnostic audit — recorded 21 Jul 2026. Prompted by a user report that "income exposed" showed $0 for the us_ccorp_indian_sub profile despite real business income on file. The single-field fix (Monitor's incomeExposedUsd) was correct but symptomatic of a much larger pattern: this whole product was built and verified almost entirely against individual/HUF taxpayers, and a US or India entity taxpayer silently breaks several genuinely computational paths, not just display fields — directly relevant to H.1-H.4 above, since a wrong FTC relief figure or a "$NaN" tax-computation trace is exactly the kind of thing H.4's "Estimated — confirm before filing" marker can't paper over (it isn't an estimate, it's a bug).

Root cause. model.income.us.total/usSourceTotal/foreignSourceTotal (engine) and the DAG's mirror aggregate are INDIVIDUAL-1040-shaped (wages + interest + dividends + business_us + …), always $0 for a business entity — an entity's real income is Schedule M-1 book-to-tax reconciled (entity.usScheduleM1TaxableIncomeUsd → computed.usTax.totalIncomeUsd), a completely separate data path Layer 1 never folds into the generic aggregate. Six distinct places read that always-$0 aggregate without checking entity routing first, corrupting real numbers (not just labels): India's own s.90 FTC relief for US tax paid, the FY↔CY Apportionment card, the income summary/headline total, the US Tax Computation trace (a literal "$NaN" in user-facing formula text — and its exact India-side analog, "₹NaN", on the entity tax-computation trace), the underpayment_2210 finding (cites Form 2210/§6654, an individual-only statute, on a corporation), and CL-2's calendar-amounts feature (same wrong statute). India's own income aggregate was NOT affected — India's business-entry pipeline is shared between individual and company/firm taxpayers, so entity PGBP income already flowed through correctly there.

Decision on how to fix it. The engine (engine/.js) is frozen per the DAG-migration policy (docs/DAG_MIGRATION_TRACKER.md) and these are engine-rooted bugs. Since engine and DAG are now numerically identical (SYS-3 closed, byte-for-byte parity fully proven across ~10,000 fuzzed profiles), the byte-identical invariant no longer needs to be maintained as a goal in itself — explicit product direction (21 Jul 2026): *fix every one of these bugs correctly in the DAG only, never touch engine/.js, and update the fuzzer/shadow-mode/test-adapter tooling to allow this specific, catalogued set of known, intentional divergences.* The DAG is now the more-correct implementation for a US-entity, India-entity, or NRA taxpayer on exactly the paths below; everywhere else, and for every individual/HUF taxpayer, byte-identical parity is still required and still enforced.

What was fixed, and where (all in prototypes/graph-pilot/-nodes.js, synced to monitor-next/lib/dag/):*

  • ustax-full-nodes.js's usEntityResult() (the C-Corp/S-Corp/partnership/trust tax result): foreignSourceIncomeUsd/usSourceIncomeUsd now derived from the entity's own taxable amount instead of the individual aggregate.
  • ustax-full-nodes.js's apportionmentResult override: usCyTotalUsd uses the entity-aware figure only when usTaxResult.isEntity — an early, broader version of this fix swapped it in unconditionally and introduced a real new divergence for FEIE-electing individuals and NRAs (both legitimately narrower than the raw aggregate for unrelated, correct reasons); caught by run-fuzz.js and narrowed back to entity-only.
  • xborder-full-nodes.js's usSourceTotalUsdBoundaryFtc: now reads the routed usTaxResult.usSourceIncomeUsd. Fixed India's own s.90 FTC relief for a US entity, and incidentally fixed a latent NRA-side inconsistency in the same boundary (confirmed a genuine additional correctness improvement, not a regression).
  • agg10-nodes.js's headlineResult: totalIncomeUsd now sums the entity's own taxable income when usTaxResult.isEntity, instead of silently dropping it.
  • agg10-nodes.js's us1ShouldFire override: underpayment_2210 (Form 2210/§6654) suppressed for a US entity — a corporation's estimated-tax underpayment is Form 2220/§6655, a distinct, unmodeled regime; showing nothing is more correct than showing the wrong form's number.
  • calendar-amounts-nodes.js: calendarAmountsResult.us.installments is [] for a US entity, same reasoning, mirroring the pre-existing !inAdvTaxObliged gate already used on the India side.
  • report-batch2-nodes.js's buildTaxComputationUsResult: a genuine entity branch (Taxable income → flat/pass-through tax → Total) replaces the individual row set the engine reuses verbatim for an entity, which is where its "$NaN" comes from (ordinaryTaxableUsd, saltCapUsd, etc. are all undefined on the entity result object).
  • report-batch3-nodes.js's buildTaxComputationIndiaResult: same fix, India side — a genuine entity branch (Taxable income → tax at entity/MAT rate → surcharge → cess → total), fixing the exact "₹NaN" analog (i.totalNormalInr interpolated unconditionally into a slab-rate formula string that only individual returns populate).

Tooling updated to allow the (now-deliberate, catalogued) divergences instead of failing on them: prototypes/graph-pilot/run-fuzz.js (new "Section D" allowlist — KNOWN_US_ENTITY_DIVERGENT_PATHS/KNOWN_INDIA_ENTITY_DIVERGENT_PATHS/KNOWN_NRA_DIVERGENT_PATHS, plus a named exception in compareFindings for underpayment_2210), run-agg10.js, run-report3.js, run-analyze.js, run-ustax-full.js, run-calendaramounts.js (each gated strictly on the profile's actual taxpayer shape, never on label), and monitor-next/test-adapter.mjs / monitor-next/lib/shadow-core.js (same allowlist convention, so shadow mode in the running app doesn't flag these as live discrepancies). Every allowlist entry is scoped to the specific field paths actually affected — verified via run-fuzz.js at 5,000+ randomly generated profiles per seed with zero unexplained divergences remaining, and the full run-*.js harness suite (40+ files) green.

Known remaining gap, not fixed here (out of scope — a data-modeling gap, not a wiring bug): the Reconciliation view's "US income — by head" card still reads the individual-shaped aggregate directly and shows "No US income on file" for a US entity, inconsistent with the real $4.26M figure the Tax Computation card right below it now correctly shows. Modeling a real by-head breakdown of an entity's Schedule M-1 income is a genuine new feature (Layer 1 US Business currently collects only the aggregate), not a bug fix — flagged here rather than silently left inconsistent.

H.6 — Business entity coverage, Phase 1: silently-wrong entity routing — recorded 21 Jul 2026

Follow-on to H.5. User direction: "we have made everything possible from the POV of an individual but we haven't even touched the business part yet... there are so many entity types apart from individual." A scan of both sides' entity-type enums found two MORE cases where a real, selectable Layer 1 entity type was silently taxed as a plain individual — not a display bug like H.5's "$NaN", but the entity paying the wrong tax entirely, with no error or flag. Phase 1 (of a 3-phase plan: this routing fix, then US state income tax, then a real multi-entity/entity-graph model) closes these two. Both fixes are DAG-only (prototypes/graph-pilot, synced to monitor-next/lib/dag), per the same standing "engine frozen, fix the DAG" policy H.5 established — engine/*.js untouched.

H.6.1 — India: AOP/BOI and Trust/NGO/Political Party taxed as a plain individual. Layer 1 India's entity_type dropdown has 8 real values: individual, huf, firm, llp, company, aop, trust, ajp, local. normalize.js's routing (if (E.indiaIsCompany || E.indiaIsFirm)) only recognizes company and firm/llp/local — aop and trust fall through to the individual/HUF slab computation (standard deduction, §156/§87A rebate, the works). That's correct for huf and defensible for ajp (both genuinely use individual-style slab rates under Indian law), but wrong for the other two:

  • AOP/BOI (s.167B): taxed at the Maximum Marginal Rate (42.744% = 30% top slab × 1.37 old-regime top surcharge × 1.04 cess) whenever member shares are indeterminate — or determinate but any member's own income exceeds the basic exemption, the common case for a real commercial AOP. Layer 1 collects no member-share-determinacy facts at all, so MMR (the statutory default for the unestablished case, not a guess) now applies unconditionally. Fixed in entitytax-nodes.js.
  • Trust/NGO/Political Party: the engine's OWN computeIndiaItrForm (computation.js:1753-1756, ported unchanged in itrform-nodes.js) already recommends ITR-7 "claiming tax exemptions under Trust & NGO Tax Exemptions" for this exact entity_type value — i.e. the engine's own form-determination logic already assumes s.11/12A (charitable) or s.13A (political party) exemption, while computeIndiaEntityTax computed a nonzero individual-slab tax on the same income, directly contradicting its own ITR-7 recommendation. Aligned in the DAG: treated as exemption-claiming (tax = 0), with the compliance conditions (85% application-of-income test, valid 12A/12AB registration, s.13A's books/audit/cash-donation conditions) named as unverified rather than silently assumed met. A genuine private/family trust (not claiming any exemption) is a different real-world case this Layer 1 field cannot currently distinguish from the charitable/political case — not solved here, recorded as a further known gap.

Both routing fixes cascade into isEntityTaxpayer, model.entity.isBusiness/indiaReturnForm (AOP → ITR-5, Trust → ITR-7), the model.assets.businessEntities[] return-form trace, and — for AOP specifically, since its tax AMOUNT is now genuinely different, not just its trace — nearly everything India-tax-derived: FTC both directions, headline/summary totals, several finding IDs (ftc_gap/promoter_buyback_additional_tax/carry_forward_losses_not_applied all correctly disappear for an exempt trust), monitoring health. Every touched site (8 files: entitytax-nodes.js, india-full-nodes.js, india-tax-combined-nodes.js, agg10-nodes.js, assets-nodes.js, checks-registry-nodes.js, findings-batch3-nodes.js, report-batch1-nodes.js) was individually reviewed, not blanket-widened — e.g. the company-specific POEM-mismatch finding correctly stayed company-only, since AOP/Trust have no incorporation-place residency test in the same sense.

H.6.2 — US: a trust (Form 1041) always computed $0 entity-level tax, even when it should owe real (and highly compressed) bracket tax. computeUsEntityTax treated a trust identically to an S-corp/partnership: 100% pass-through, $0 at the entity level. Investigation found this was actually defensible for a fully-distributing trust (a real "simple trust" under §651 genuinely owes ~$0 at the entity level — the distribution deduction offsets DNI) — but Layer 1 US had no field at all for the case that should NOT be $0: a trust that retains (accumulates) income. Its only trust-income field is framed as "Beneficiaries' Share of Income," implying full distribution, and corporate_financials.schedule_m1 is explicitly gated to ccorp/scorp/partnership only — never populated for trust.

Closed with one new Layer 1 US field, not a caveat-only fix (user's explicit choice over a smaller, no-new-field option): "Income retained by the trust (not allocated to any beneficiary this year)", added to the trust profile step (layer1_us.html, usState.profile.trust_retained_income_usd, defaults to 0). Wired through ustax-full-nodes.js's usEntityTaxResult:

  • Distributed portion (the existing "beneficiaries' share" aggregate) stays untaxed at the entity level — taxed on the beneficiaries' own returns instead, same as before.
  • Retained portion is taxed at the real §1(e) trust/estate brackets — far more compressed than the individual brackets (37% starts around $15,650 vs. $640,600+ for a single individual). TY2025 figures (Rev. Proc. 2024-40) are the best-available confirmed numbers at hand; TY2026's exact Rev. Proc. 2025-32 trust/estate table hasn't been independently re-verified, flagged as such in-code rather than asserted as final.
  • totalIncomeUsd/usSourceIncomeUsd report the FULL economic total (distributed + retained) for cross-border FTC/apportionment purposes, which want "how much did this entity earn," not "how much is taxed at its level" — only taxableIncomeUsd narrows to the retained slice.
  • report-batch2-nodes.js's Tax Computation trace gained a genuine trust-specific row set (total → distributed (not taxed here) → retained → bracket tax → total), replacing the flat-rate/pass-through shape that doesn't fit a bracket computation.

Verified: hand-computed bracket math on a $25,000-retained synthetic case matches the DAG exactly ($7,236.50); browser-confirmed live (both the new Layer 1 US field — visibility toggle, value persistence — and the Reconciliation view's resulting trace).

H.6.3 — Verification. run-fuzz.js's existing entity-type mutation pool already generates aop/trust/coop (India) and trust (US tax_entity_type) — extended its Section D allowlist with KNOWN_INDIA_AOP_TRUST_DIVERGENT_PATHS and KNOWN_US_TRUST_DIVERGENT_PATHS (each gated strictly on the profile's actual model.entity shape) plus new DAG_ONLY_KEYS entries for the fields with no engine equivalent at all (indiaIsAop/indiaIsTrust/trustDistributedUsd/trustRetainedUsd/trustBracketBreakdown) — mirrored into shadow-core.js and test-adapter.mjs. Clean across 4 fuzz seeds / 13,000+ generated profiles with zero unexplained divergences, full run-*.js harness suite (40+ files) green, test-adapter.mjs/test-shadow.mjs green (neither exercises AOP/trust today since none of the 11 real demo profiles use those entity types — coverage here is from the fuzzer and direct synthetic tests, not the fixed-fixture harnesses). coop (India entity_type) appears in the fuzzer's mutation pool but is NOT a real, reachable Layer 1 dropdown value — noted so it isn't mistaken for a live gap.

Not done in Phase 1 (Phases 2-3, separate work): US state income tax (still $0 everywhere, blocks real multi-state entity apportionment) and a genuine multi-entity/entity-graph model (today the product analyzes one taxpayer at a time; an individual who owns an entity AND has personal K-1 income elsewhere has no connected representation).

H.7 — Business entity coverage, Phase 2: US state income tax — recorded 21 Jul 2026

Follow-on to H.6. computeUsStateTax (computation.js:1133-1182) — the engine's only state-tax computation — had two real gaps, one on the individual side and one on the entity side, both closed here, DAG-only, engine/*.js untouched:

H.7.1 — Individual: only CA/NY modeled; every other state, including the two genuinely tax-free ones and the top "NRI top-5" states, silently returned nothing. A prior GAP_TRACKER pass ("US-12") had this row down as "$0 everywhere," which was stale — computeUsStateTax already modeled CA and NY correctly. The real gap was narrower but still real: US_STATES (constants.js) has exactly two entries, so a taxpayer domiciled in any other state got a silent null — indistinguishable, from the UI, between "genuinely zero" and "not computed." Two concrete cases closed in findings-batch5-nodes.js's usStateTaxResult:

  • New Jersey: a real bracket table added (US_STATES_NJ_NY_SHAPE_EXT), same shape as CA/NY (BRACKETS/STD_DEDUCTION/DEPENDENT_EXEMPTION_USD) so report-batch2-nodes.js's existing trace renderer needed no structural changes — only its hardcoded CA/NY label text genericized via new standardDeductionLabel/dependentExemptionLabel fields (NY's own exact original wording — "Less NY dependent exemption ($1,000/dependent)" — preserved byte-for-byte via an explicit state === "NY" branch, so this genericization introduces zero avoidable divergence from the engine on the one state the engine already covers). TY2025 NJ Div. of Taxation brackets (unchanged for several years) — best-available figures, not independently re-verified for the exact TY2026 vintage.
  • AK/FL/NV/SD/TN/TX/WA/WY (NO_INDIVIDUAL_INCOME_TAX_STATES): states with genuinely no individual income tax. Previously indistinguishable from "not computed" — both silently returned null. Now returns an explicit { noIncomeTax: true, totalTaxUsd: 0, basis: "<State> has no individual income tax." } result instead of nothing, surfaced in report-batch2-nodes.js's trace as a short confirmation card rather than the full bracket-walk shape, and in checks-registry-nodes.js's state_income_tax pass-check text (distinguishing "no income tax at all" from "nets to zero at this state's own rates").

Every other individual-side US state remains genuinely unmodeled — not claimed otherwise.

H.7.2 — Entity: computeUsStateTax excludes ALL business entities unconditionally (computation.js:1129-1132's own comment: business entities "file separate state franchise/entity-level returns, an unrelated and unmodeled regime, so this function only fires for individual filers"), meaning a US C-Corp, S-Corp, partnership, or trust got the exact same silent null regardless of state — no distinction between "this state has real, unmodeled entity-level exposure" and "this entity genuinely owes nothing." Closed with a new usEntityStateTaxResult node (ustax-full-nodes.js, reusing the pre-existing shared usState.profile.state_of_domicile Layer 1 field — already collected for entities via the corp-domicile select, so no new Layer 1 field was needed here unlike H.6.2's trust field) at the same "planning-grade, single-state, no apportionment" fidelity as the individual brackets, with everything else honestly flagged unmodeled rather than defaulted to zero:

  • C-Corp in CA/NY/NJ: a real, if simplified, entity-level income tax — flat top-bracket rate (CA 8.84% is genuinely flat; NY's 7.25% and NJ's 9% are each real top brackets with the lower-bracket/minimum-tax/surtax detail explicitly excluded and named in the finding text), computed on the entity's own federal taxable income (usEntityTaxResult.taxableIncomeUsd).
  • S-Corp/Partnership (any state): pass-through at the state level too, same as federal — no entity-level computation, but flagged that a state PTET (pass-through entity tax) election isn't checked.
  • Trust (any state): state fiduciary income tax has its own throwback/accumulation-distribution rules, materially different from the federal §1(e) brackets in H.6.2 — not modeled, flagged as such.
  • TX/WA (any entity kind): deliberately NOT given the individual side's "$0, confirmed no tax" treatment — Texas's Franchise (Margin) Tax and Washington's B&O tax are real, gross-receipts/margin-based taxes with no income-tax analog to reuse the bracket model for. The finding names the actual regime and explicitly says not to assume $0.
  • Every other state: genuinely not modeled, same honest "don't assume $0" framing.

Surfaced as two new finding IDs only (us_entity_state_tax when a real amount is computed, us_entity_state_tax_not_modeled for every caveated case) — not a new taxComputation/document card, to avoid a frontend schema change for a first cut; the Monitor already renders findings generically. Verified live in the browser: injecting a CA domicile onto the us_ccorp_indian_sub demo profile via localStorage + Refresh renders "California state entity-level tax: $376,584 (C-Corp)" with correct detail/recommendation/refs text (hand-verified: $4,260,000 × 8.84% = $376,584); a TX domicile instead renders the Franchise Tax caveat finding, no crash, no console errors.

H.7.3 — Verification. run-fuzz.js's KNOWN_EXTRA_FINDING_ID regex now allowlists both new finding IDs (previously /^$/, matching nothing — this is its first real use). form_nj1040 (the new NJ document, no engine equivalent since NJ was never modeled before) required stripping from documents[] and every monitoring.calendar.{all,upcoming,next}[].docIds array across run-fuzz.js, run-report1.js, run-analyze.js, run-monitor.js, test-adapter.mjs, and shadow-core.js — Date-safe shallow copies (Object.assign), not a full JSON clone, everywhere a monitoring tree was touched. Clean across 4 fuzz seeds / 8,500+ generated profiles with zero unexplained divergences, full run-*.js harness suite (40+ files) green, test-adapter.mjs (10,382 checks) and test-shadow.mjs green. The entity-state-tax finding doesn't fire on any of the 11 real demo profiles or SAMPLE (none sets state_of_domicile on an entity) — coverage here is from the direct synthetic tests above and manual fuzzer-style injection, not the fixed-fixture harnesses, same caveat H.6.3 recorded for AOP/trust.

Not done in Phase 2 (Phase 3, separate work): a genuine multi-entity/entity-graph model — today the product analyzes one taxpayer at a time; an individual who owns an entity AND has personal K-1 income elsewhere has no connected representation, and inter-entity flows (K-1s, dividends, GILTI/Subpart F, §962) aren't traced as edges.

H.8 — Business entity coverage, Phase 3 Tier 1: ownership graph + Entity Structure tab — recorded 21 Jul 2026

Follow-on to H.6/H.7. The user's framing for this whole 3-phase effort ("we haven't even touched the business part... there are so many entity types apart from individual") named a genuine multi-entity/entity-graph model as its final piece — today the product analyzes ONE taxpayer's {router, india, us} tuple at a time; an individual who owns a separate legal entity, and that entity itself, are two unrelated rows in the Client Portfolio with no representation that one owns the other. engine/profiles.js itself already flagged this gap in a comment on founder_indian_company: "Full entity separation arrives with multi-entity Phase 1." Given the size jump from Phases 1-2 (each a bounded computation fix) to a real graph model, this phase was explicitly scoped into 3 tiers before any code was written, and only Tier 1 was built in this pass — Tier 2 (deriving K-1/dividend flow amounts from the linked entity's own computed results, replacing manual entry) and Tier 3 (real GILTI/Subpart F/§962 computation) are deliberately not started, pending review.

What Tier 1 is: a pure client-portfolio-level concept — new code lives entirely in monitor-next/lib/entity-graph.js and components/Views.jsx's new EntityStructureView, NOT in prototypes/graph-pilot or engine/*.js. There is no engine equivalent to keep DAG-parity with (the frozen engine has zero concept of one taxpayer owning another), so the usual fuzzer/shadow-mode/run-fuzz.js verification discipline doesn't apply here — this is genuinely new product surface, not a computation fix.

  • ENTITY_LINKS (entity-graph.js): a small array of { ownerId, ownedId, ownershipPct, relationship, note } records connecting two existing WISING.PROFILES client ids by ownership. One seed link for this pass: founder_indian_company (100%, "CFC shareholder (>10% — Form 5471/GILTI)") → india_pvt_ltd.
  • entityLinksFor(clientId, allSummaries): pure lookup, no new engine/DAG calls — reuses whichever clientSummaries array is already in state (from allClientSummaries() or allClientSummariesDag(), same shape either way), so it stays correct under either compute-source toggle with no extra plumbing. Returns null for the common case (a client with no links at all) rather than an empty-but-truthy shape, so the UI can distinguish "nothing linked" from "linked to nothing." Single-hop only (deliberately not recursive yet — see the follow-up note below).
  • EntityStructureView (new "Structure" sidebar tab, between Monitor and Residency): for the active client, shows their own combined summary, an "Owned by" section (incoming links) and an "Owns" section (outgoing links) — each linked entity rendered with its OWN already-computed combined tax/health score/conflict count (not re-derived, just displayed), the relationship/ownership%, an "Open →" link that switches the active client straight into that entity's own Monitor (reusing the existing pickFromClients handler), and the link's caveat note where present. A sidebar badge shows the active client's link count.

Deliberately NOT done in Tier 1 (no new tax computation): the GILTI figure shown for founder_indian_company is still exactly the same hand-entered Layer 1 estimate it always was ($108,433 per profiles.js) — Tier 1 only makes it visible ALONGSIDE india_pvt_ltd's own real, separately-computed India tax return, not derived FROM it. No K-1/dividend/Subpart-F amount changes anywhere as a result of this phase.

Post-ship user review found the one seed link read as nonsensical, and fixed it — a genuine, narrow exception to the frozen-engine policy, made only with explicit user sign-off. First cut: founder_indian_company's own hand-entered CFC label ("Nova Systems Pvt Ltd", us.foreign_entities.foreign_corporations[0].corporation_name) didn't match india_pvt_ltd's actual entity name ("Nimbus Analytics Pvt Ltd") — I'd linked them anyway, as the closest available pairing among the 11 fixed demo profiles (no advisor-facing UI exists yet to create a real link from scratch), and tried to paper over the mismatch with an explanatory note. The user correctly called this out as making no sense on its face. Presented with the actual choice — rename the entity everywhere (requires editing engine/profiles.js, breaking the "engine frozen" rule that's governed every change across Phases 1-3) vs. a display-only alias scoped to just this one view — the user explicitly chose the everywhere rename, so india_pvt_ltd's entity name was changed from "Nimbus Analytics Pvt Ltd" to "Nova Systems Pvt Ltd" throughout engine/profiles.js (4 data fields + 1 code comment; label/story/tags never mentioned the old name, so untouched). Text-only — no computed figure, bracket, or rate changed — and since both the engine and the DAG read this name from the same ctx.india.profile.full_name/router fields rather than either side hardcoding it, renaming it in the one frozen source location kept both sides in identical agreement automatically; the full harness suite, run-fuzz.js (2,500 profiles/seed 11), test-adapter.mjs, and test-shadow.mjs were all re-run clean after the change with zero new divergences. Earlier tracker rows (IN-33, IN-34, XB-28) that predate this rename still say "Nimbus Analytics" — left as-is, since they're an accurate historical record of what the profile was called at the time of THOSE fixes, not a live reference; this row is the pointer for anyone who greps for "Nimbus" and comes up empty. The link's own note text was also rewritten to stop describing a discrepancy that no longer exists — it now correctly frames the link as an advisor-asserted relationship (which it structurally still is — WISING doesn't auto-match entities across profiles by name or anything else) rather than a system-flagged mismatch.

Also raised in the same review, not yet acted on: the user suggested the ownership structure should render as a genuinely NESTED/hierarchical tree, not flat "Owns"/"Owned by" lists, to avoid this kind of confusion at a deeper ownership chain (A owns B owns C). entityLinksFor is single-hop by design today — with only one link in the demo data there's nothing yet to visually nest (a depth-1 tree looks identical to a flat list) — so this wasn't built speculatively. Recursive resolution (with cycle protection, since a real ownership graph can't be assumed acyclic) plus a genuine nested-tree UI component is the natural next step once either Tier 2 work or a second demo link creates an actual multi-level chain to render.

Verified: production build (next build) compiles clean; browser-confirmed both directions of the one seed link (founder_indian_company's "Owns" section showing india_pvt_ltd at 100%/$184,666/health 97 — now correctly labeled "Nova Systems Pvt Ltd" throughout, and india_pvt_ltd's "Owned by" section showing the reverse), the empty state for an unlinked client (dual_resident_h1b), the "Open →" cross-navigation (correctly switches the active client AND jumps to their Monitor), and the sidebar badge — no console errors in any case. test-adapter.mjs (10,382 checks) and test-shadow.mjs re-confirmed green after both the initial ship and the rename.

Not done in Tier 1 (Tiers 2-3, pending a check-in before starting): deriving K-1/dividend/GILTI amounts from the linked entity's own computed results instead of manual entry (Tier 2); real GILTI/Subpart F computation (tested income, QBAI from the CFC's own asset data — which india_pvt_ltd-shaped profiles already capture via asset_blocks) and §962 election modeling (Tier 3); a UI mechanism for an advisor to CREATE a new ownership link themselves (today ENTITY_LINKS is hardcoded demo data, not editable from any Layer 0/1 form); and any handling of ownership chains deeper than one hop (not exercised by the single seed link, though entityLinksFor's per-id lookup would need to become recursive with cycle protection for a real multi-level chain).

H.9 — Move constants.js/profiles.js/sample-data.js out of engine/ into the DAG — recorded 21 Jul 2026

Prompted by user review of the H.8 rename fix: the user asked why fixing india_pvt_ltd's entity name required editing anything under engine/ at all, given the "engine frozen, changes go in the DAG" policy that's governed the whole business-entity effort. The precise answer — docs/DAG_MIGRATION_TRACKER.md shows all 4 COMPUTATION files (computation.js, conflicts.js, monitoring.js, normalize.js) were already fully ported into prototypes/graph-pilot (40/40 rows, done before this session), but constants.js/profiles.js/sample-data.js are reference DATA, never ported, and never duplicated on purpose (SYS-1's own resolution: a single shared import, specifically to prevent the two-copies-silently-drift-apart risk a naive duplication would reintroduce) — still left these 3 files sitting under engine/, so any change to demo-client data meant touching a folder the whole project had been treating as untouchable. User's explicit direction: move them into the DAG's own territory.

What moved: engine/constants.js → prototypes/graph-pilot/constants.js, same for profiles.js and sample-data.js (git-tracked renames, full history preserved). All 14 DAG node files that read tax constants (aggregateindiaincome-nodes.js, aggregateusincome-nodes.js, assets-nodes.js, checks-registry-nodes.js, entitytax-nodes.js, findings-batch3/4/5/6-nodes.js, in1-nodes-v3.js, limits-nodes.js, report-batch1-nodes.js, ustax-full-nodes.js, ustax-nodes.js) now require("./constants.js") — a same-directory sibling — instead of reaching into ../../engine/. prototypes/graph-pilot now has zero references to engine/ anywhere in its own code (verified: grep -rl "\.\./\.\./engine" prototypes/graph-pilot/.js matches nothing outside the run-.js test harnesses, which legitimately still load the real engine for differential-testing purposes — that's their entire job, not a leftover dependency).

What did NOT move, and why engine/ still has all 7 files on disk: a large existing surface expects a complete, classic 7-file engine/ folder exactly where it's always been — router.html's own <script src="engine/constants.js"> tags, every one of the ~40 run-.js test harnesses' ["constants", "normalize", ..., "profiles"].forEach(...) loader, tests/engine/run.js, npm run audit:dag (scripts/audit/dag-coverage.js, reads engine/.js as its comparison baseline), and monitor-next's OWN sync-engine.js (copies all 7 files from engine/ into lib/engine//public/engine/ for the live product's "Engine" fallback mode). Rewriting all of that to look somewhere else would have been a much larger, much riskier change for zero real benefit — these consumers only need the DATA to be present at that path, not authored there. Instead, a new script, scripts/sync-fixtures-to-engine.js, copies the 3 moved files from prototypes/graph-pilot/ back into engine/ as clearly-marked generated mirrors (/ GENERATED — mirrored from prototypes/graph-pilot/X.js... Do not hand-edit this copy / header on each). This is the exact same "single source + generated downstream copies" pattern the project already uses for engine/ → lib/engine/ → public/engine/ — just one hop further upstream, not a new architecture. engine/constants.js/profiles.js/sample-data.js are build output now, same category as lib/engine/.js and public/engine/.js — never hand-edited directly, regenerated by running the script (wired into monitor-next's predev/prebuild, ahead of its own sync-engine.js, which needs these files present in engine/ first).

monitor-next/scripts/sync-dag.js simplified as a side effect: it used to rewrite each copied DAG file's require("../../engine/constants.js") string to require("../engine/constants.js") on the way into lib/dag/ (since lib/dag and lib/engine are siblings, two levels up from prototypes/graph-pilot's own position at the repo root). Now that DAG node files reference ./constants.js as a same-directory sibling, and sync-dag.js already copies every non-run-* .js file in prototypes/graph-pilot/ (which now includes constants.js/profiles.js/sample-data.js themselves), the whole rewrite step is unnecessary — a plain file copy keeps every reference correct automatically, since constants.js lands in lib/dag/ right alongside everything that needs it.

Verified: node tests/engine/run.js (78/78), the full run-*.js harness suite (40+ files, all pass, including from a completely fresh git mv), run-fuzz.js/npm run fuzz:dag (2000+ profiles, 2 fresh seeds, 0 new divergences), npm run audit:dag (zero unexpected gaps, zero numeric drift, 58/58 finding IDs), monitor-next's test-adapter.mjs (10,382 checks) and test-shadow.mjs, a clean npm run build from a wiped lib/dag/lib/engine (forcing the full predev/prebuild chain including the new fixture-sync step to run from nothing), and browser-verified with headless Chromium: the Monitor app loads with zero console errors, router.html (the one page that <script src>s engine/constants.js/sample-data.js/profiles.js directly, not through any bundler) loads and renders its title correctly, /engine/constants.js and /engine/profiles.js both serve 200 from the deployed static mirror, and the Entity Structure tab (H.8) still renders "Nova Systems Pvt Ltd" and its linked $184,666 figure correctly post-move.

Not done (explicitly, per a separate direct question this same session): retiring engine/computation.js/conflicts.js/monitoring.js/normalize.js themselves. Those 4 files remain the differential-testing reference baseline every run-*.js harness, the fuzzer, and shadow mode check the DAG against — removing them would collapse that entire verification strategy (there'd be nothing independent left to diff against, and the fuzzer's auto-generated-input coverage in particular has no real substitute without a second implementation to check against). This move only relocates DATA the DAG needed to stop reaching into engine/ for; the actual frozen reference LOGIC is untouched and still serves its original purpose.

H.10 — Business entity coverage, Phase 3: nest owned entities in the Clients tab — recorded 21 Jul 2026

Follow-on to H.8. The Entity Structure tab (a separate page) made ownership links visible, but the user pointed out a real remaining confusion: someone browsing the main Client Portfolio table would see an individual and the company they own as two disconnected rows with no visual indication of the relationship at all, unless they specifically knew to go look at the Structure tab. Explicit ask: nest the owned entity directly under its owner in the Clients table itself, expandable via a click, and — importantly — build this now even though there's only one link in the demo data today ("even if there is one level tree too"), not wait for a deeper chain to justify it.

What changed, components/Views.jsx's ClientsView: a client row that owns at least one linked entity (entityLinksFor, per-row, same helper H.8 built) now renders a ▸ twisty arrow in its name cell. Clicking it (not the row itself — stopPropagation keeps the two independent) expands an indented child row directly beneath, showing the owned entity's own already-computed summary (same columns as any other row: type, residency, combined tax, FTC residual, conflicts, health, next filing) plus a small badge with the ownership % and relationship type. Clicking the nested row navigates exactly like any other row (onPick, same handler) — confirmed this opens the linked entity's own Monitor, not some special sub-view.

Built recursive from the start, not flat-and-special-cased for one hop: buildRows(client, depth, link, visited) renders a client's row, and — only if currently expanded — recursively renders rows for each of ITS OWNED entities one level deeper, with its own arrow if that entity in turn owns something. A table can't nest <tr> inside <tr>, so this flattens the tree into a plain indented sequence (depth * 22px left padding + a ↳ glyph) rather than an actual nested DOM structure. visited (a Set threaded through the recursion) stops a cycle in the ownership data from becoming infinite recursion — not reachable with today's single seed link, but the graph itself doesn't guarantee acyclic, so this is real protection, not decoration, and means a second or third link added later needs zero UI changes to render correctly at whatever depth it creates.

Follow-up, same session: user confirmed the duplicate top-level row DID read as confusing — fixed. india_pvt_ltd no longer appears in its own top-level sorted position; ownedIds (every id that's the ownedId of some link, computed once per render from the full clients array) is filtered OUT of sorted before the table renders, so each real filing shows exactly once — at the top level if unowned, nested under its owner otherwise. It's still fully reachable (nested, one click away) and still counted in the underlying clients data — only the flat top-level ROW LIST changed. The 5 KPI tiles above the table (Clients/At risk/Open critical/Combined tax/FTC residual) deliberately still SUM the full book including nested entities — hiding a real entity's tax/conflict numbers from the totals just because it's now shown nested would under-report actual exposure, a worse failure than the row-duplication this fix addresses. Since that creates a real gap between "Clients: 11" and "10 rows visible," the Clients tile gained a clarifying sub-label ("10 shown · 1 nested under an owner") rather than leaving the discrepancy unexplained. Verified: 10 top-level rows collapsed, 11 after expanding founder_indian_company, exactly 1 occurrence of "Indian Pvt Ltd (company)" inside the table (down from 2), no console errors.

Verified: production build compiles clean; browser-confirmed the arrow only appears on rows with a real owned-entity link (Dual Resident — H-1B, no link, correctly has zero buttons in its row), expanding adds exactly one row (11 → 12), collapsing removes it (12 → 11), the nested row's data and badge render correctly ("↳ Indian Pvt Ltd (company)", "100% · CFC shareholder (>10% — Form 5471 / GILTI)", $184,666, health 97), and clicking the nested row navigates to "Nova Systems Pvt Ltd"'s own Monitor exactly like a normal row click — no console errors throughout.

H.11 — Business entity coverage, Phase 3: the 3 remaining nesting-consistency gaps — recorded 21 Jul 2026

Follow-on to H.10. Once the Clients tab nested owned entities, the user asked whether that was the only place nesting mattered — it wasn't; 3 more UI surfaces still showed the flat, unlinked picture, and the user asked for all 3 built. All 3 are pure monitor-next UI changes (no engine/DAG file touched), verified in both ?engine=dag (default) and ?engine=engine modes.

1. Header "Switch client…" dropdown, now nested. New lib/entity-graph.js helpers — ownedEntityIds(items) (every id that's the ownedId of some link, factored out of the same logic H.10 already had inline) and flattenOwnershipTree(orderedRoots, items) (a general-purpose, non-interactive version of H.10's buildRows — always shows every descendant, no expand/collapse state, since a <select> has no interaction model for that) — work against listProfiles()'s lightweight {id, label, story, tags} shape, not just the full clientSummaries shape, since that's all the header dropdown has. app/page.jsx's orderedProfiles (memoized on profiles) reorders the options so Indian Pvt Ltd (company) sits immediately after Founder · Indian company, prefixed with a ↳ and non-breaking-space indentation (regular spaces get collapsed by the browser's normal whitespace rendering inside <option> text;   doesn't) — and no longer appears at its own separate position in the list, matching H.10's "show it once" rule. Verified: <option> text content and ordering read correctly via direct DOM inspection (native <select> popups don't screenshot in headless Chromium, so this was checked via $$eval rather than a screenshot).

2. Compliance Calendar + Documents, now merged across owned entities. New lib/wising.js export analyzeProfileById(id) and lib/dag-adapter.js export analyzeProfileByIdDag(id) — a full analyze()/analyzeDag() call for a SPECIFIC profile id regardless of which client is currently active (needed because the calendar/documents aren't part of the lightweight clientSummaries shape H.8's Structure tab and H.10's nesting both got away with using). app/page.jsx's linkedFilings (memoized on activeLinks/engineSource) resolves the active client's OWNED entities' full results and passes them to FilingsView as a new linkedEntities prop. FilingsView merges each linked entity's monitoring.calendar.all and documents into the active client's own, appending " — <entity name>" to every merged-in row/document's .name (every calendar rendering mode — Timeline/Calendar/List — and the documents grid all just render .name directly, so this is the one attribution point that propagates everywhere with no changes to those 3 renderer components) and re-sorting by date. A real correctness risk surfaced and fixed while building this: DeadlineDetailModal's "filed with this deadline" lookup used to filter the WHOLE passed-in docs array by id membership in the clicked deadline's own docIds — fine for one entity, but two different linked entities can legitimately share a catalog document id (both file their own Form 8938, say), so a naive merge would show the WRONG entity's document (or both) in the popup. Fixed by attaching entityDocs to each calendar row at merge time (the active client's own rows point to its own documents; each linked entity's rows point to THAT entity's own documents), and having the modal prefer x.entityDocs || docs — scopes the lookup correctly per row regardless of how many entities are merged in. Only the active client's OUTGOING (owns) links roll up, matching H.10's nesting direction — viewing the owned entity's own Filings tab does not pull the owner's personal deadlines back in. Verified: merge banner + "— Indian Pvt Ltd (company)" tagged rows render correctly in both compute-source modes, required-document count rises from the individual's own figure to include the linked entity's (18 total in the founder_indian_company case), no console errors.

Observed but not addressed this pass, flagged for the user rather than acted on unprompted: the "Documents to File" grid roughly doubles in length once a link is merged in, since it shows every catalog entry (including the many N/A/dimmed ones) for BOTH entities — a lot of visual noise for what's mostly duplicate boilerplate. A follow-up could filter the merged-in portion to required-only.

3. "Also owns X" breadcrumb on Monitor, Business, and Holdings. New components/Views.jsx export OwnedEntitiesBanner({ links, onPick }) — a compact pointer rendered when links.owns has at least one resolved entry, naming each owned entity (with its ownership %) as a clickable button that calls onPick (the same pickFromClients handler every other cross-navigation in the app already uses) to jump straight into that entity's own Monitor. Wired into: the Monitor overview (inline in page.jsx, above the alerts banner), BusinessView (both its empty-state and populated-state returns — the empty-state return existed separately and needed its own copy of the banner), and HoldingsView. Only the owns direction is surfaced (same asymmetry as everywhere else this graph shows) — being owned by someone isn't announced on your own pages. Verified in the browser: the banner appears on all 3 tabs when viewing founder_indian_company, correctly absent for an unlinked client, and clicking the entity name from the Business tab (the most natural place to notice it, since that tab already shows the same company by name in its own GILTI/CFC row) correctly navigates to "Nova Systems Pvt Ltd"'s own Monitor.

H.12 — Business entity coverage, Phase 3: explicit linked-client field + dynamic link discovery (Stage a) — recorded 21 Jul 2026

Follow-on to H.8-H.11. With nesting now consistent everywhere (H.11), the user asked the harder question directly: is the separate Structure tab still needed now that ownership shows inline, or is its real job to catch relationships the system notices on its own and asks a tax professional to confirm? Investigation confirmed WISING had no auto-detection at all — H.8's ENTITY_LINKS was one hand-typed array, the single seed relationship existed only because I'd written it down separately from the Layer 1 data that actually describes it (the same "floating, unverifiable figure" problem this whole ownership-graph feature exists to fix for GILTI/K-1 numbers — the LINK ITSELF was floating too). Planned thoroughly before building anything, with the user correcting two of my own proposals along the way: PAN/EIN identity-match was ruled out as a cross-profile matching signal (a taxpayer's own PAN/EIN and a separate entity they own are, by definition, different legal persons with different numbers — a match there would actually mean a duplicate-profile data error, a different, unrequested feature), and detection was scoped to run automatically for every client, not behind an on-demand scan button. Landed design: two matching paths — exact-name matching as the automatic detector (Stage b, not yet built) and an explicit advisor-set field as a second, always-available path (Stage a, this entry) — plus a "Suggested relationships" confirm/dismiss review UI for Stage b's candidates (also not yet built).

Stage a: linked_client_id, an explicit field an advisor sets directly on the Layer 1 entry that already names the other entity. Two fields, symmetric across BOTH jurisdictions — deliberately not US-only, after the user pointed out that scoping it to only layer1_us.html would make a purely-India-side relationship (an India entity linking to another India client, with no US side at all) untaggable:

  • US Layer 1 (layer1_us.html), Foreign Corporation card (foreign_entities.foreign_corporations[]) — new "Linked client (optional)" dropdown, crp-linked-client.
  • India Layer 1 (layer1_india.html), Unlisted Equity / "Profits from Selling Private Company Shares" card (unlisted_equity.transactions[]) — new "Linked client (optional)" dropdown, ul-linked-client.

Both dropdowns list every WISING.listProfiles() entry (populated by three new <script src="engine/{constants,sample-data,profiles}.js"> tags — neither standalone Layer 1 page had ever loaded these before H.12; only router.html did) and save the selected client id straight into the entry alongside its existing fields, via each card's own existing syncCorpState()/syncUnlistedState() sync function.

monitor-next/lib/entity-graph.js rewritten from a hardcoded seed array into a live scan. discoverLinks() walks every WISING.PROFILES entry's foreign_entities.foreign_corporations[] and unlisted_equity.transactions[], picks up any entry with a linked_client_id set, and builds the link list from that — no more separately-maintained array to fall out of sync with the data it's describing. Reads whichever profile id is currently WISING.activeProfileId() from LIVE localStorage rather than the static profiles.js snapshot (rawSideFor()), so a relationship an advisor just tagged via the new dropdown shows up immediately in the Clients tab / Filings merge / Structure tab / breadcrumb — everything H.8-H.11 already built — without needing a full page reload to re-seed the static array first. ownershipPct is nullable now (India's holding_pct isn't always present on a minimal ownership-only entry, unlike the US side's ownership_percentage); the 3 render sites that display it (OwnedEntitiesBanner, the Structure tab's EntityLinkRow, the Clients tab's ClientRow) were each updated to skip the "(N%)" suffix when it's null rather than showing "(null%)" or crashing.

The one existing seed relationship (founder_indian_company → india_pvt_ltd) was re-expressed as tagged data, not carried forward as a hardcoded record. prototypes/graph-pilot/profiles.js gained linked_client_id: "india_pvt_ltd" on both founder_indian_company's foreign_corporations[0] entry and its unlisted_equity.transactions[0] entry — the same real-world relationship declared from both sides, which discoverLinks()'s de-dup (seen, keyed by ownerId→ownedId) collapses into one link, keeping whichever source it scans first.

A real, pre-existing hydration-order bug was found and fixed while browser-verifying the India-side field, unrelated to H.12's own new code but blocking proof that it worked. Loading a profile with real Financial Holdings, Commodities, or Unlisted Equity data into layer1_india.html correctly populated state on load, but the corresponding div-fin-box/div-com-box/div-ul-box stayed under their default hidden class regardless — renderFinancialHoldings()/renderCommodities()/renderUnlistedEquity() existed and correctly filled each list <div>, but nothing ever un-hid the box around it or set the fin-has/com-has/ul-has gatekeeper <select> to reflect that real data existed, unlike the equivalent prop-has/bb-has sub-modules a few lines below, which already followed the correct "render real rows, then set the gatekeeper + un-hide" sequence. Fixed by extending that exact same restore pattern to all three (restoreIndiaDOMFromState(), layer1_india.html).

That fix alone wasn't sufficient — a second, deeper bug surfaced during verification: state.unlisted_equity.transactions still ended up empty by the time renderUnlistedEquity() ran, even after the box-visibility fix, so the newly-visible card was blank instead of showing Vikram's real Nova Systems entry. Root cause, found by bisecting with temporary console logging (removed before commit): restoreIndiaDOMFromState()'s own "Run solvers" section called syncFinancialState()/syncCommodityState()/syncUnlistedState() — each of which reads its rows straight FROM the DOM (.fin-table tr / .com-card / .ul-card) — before any of those cards had been created, silently overwriting the just-hydrated state.{financial_holdings,commodities,unlisted_equity}.transactions with []. Removed those 3 calls from that early block (each render function's own addXRow() already re-derives the correct value once real rows exist, making the early call both redundant and destructive). A second, more indirect path to the same syncUnlistedState() wipe was also found and fixed at its source: evaluateUnlistedNRStatus() (invoked via runResidencySolver() → updateBizEntityType(), which fires from the very top of restoreIndiaDOMFromState()'s own Profile-section restore, long before its own Unlisted Equity render block runs) called syncUnlistedState() unconditionally on every residency evaluation; now guarded to only re-sync when at least one .ul-card actually exists in the DOM, preserving its real purpose (keeping NR-only fields in sync when a user interactively changes residency mid-session) without wiping not-yet-rendered hydration data.

Verified: full regression suite re-run clean after the profiles.js seed-link edit — node tests/engine/run.js (79/79), the full run-*.js DAG harness suite, run-fuzz.js (3000 iterations, seed 1, 0 new divergences), npm run audit:dag (no unexpected gaps), monitor-next/test-adapter.mjs (11,173 checks), monitor-next/test-shadow.mjs, and a clean npm run build from wiped lib/dag/lib/engine/public/{layer1_india,layer1_us,router}.html (forcing the full predev/prebuild chain). Browser-confirmed both directions end to end: loading founder_indian_company and opening layer1_us.html shows .crp-linked-client correctly hydrated to india_pvt_ltd ("Indian Pvt Ltd (company)"), and — after the hydration-order fixes above — opening layer1_india.html shows the Unlisted Equity box correctly un-hidden with one real card whose .ul-linked-client is likewise hydrated to india_pvt_ltd, company_name correctly populated as "Nova Systems Pvt Ltd," matching the US side exactly. No console errors in either case (excluding one pre-existing, unrelated "tailwind is not defined" warning both pages already had).

Not done (Stage b, pending): the automatic exact-name detector (should run for every client automatically, per the user's explicit direction, hooked into the existing storage-event/recompute cycle rather than a manual scan button) and the "Suggested relationships" confirm/dismiss review UI in the Structure tab for its candidates. Both deliberately deferred until Stage a — the explicit-field path this entry covers — was fully proven working end to end first.

Post-ship follow-up, same day: user reviewed the live Vercel deployment and found the two demo entries' supporting fields — Tax Year Start, Number of Shares, Acquisition Date, Cost Per Share — still blank, on top of a real deployment-hygiene problem this surfaced. The custom domain alias (test-beta-five-67.vercel.app) had been stuck pointing at an old, pre-H.12 deployment for several pushes — Vercel had already built the latest commit successfully (confirmed via the Vercel MCP connector: list_deployments/get_deployment showed a READY, target: production build off the right commit) but the specific custom alias hadn't been reassigned to it. Manually promoting that deployment in the Vercel dashboard fixed the URL; a stale browser cache of the non-hashed engine/profiles.js static asset (loaded via a plain <script src> on both standalone Layer 1 pages, unlike the Next.js app's own content-hashed bundles) explained one further round of "still not seeing it" until a hard refresh.

With the right build finally visible, the two entries' blank fields turned out to be genuine — founder_indian_company's Nova Systems entry and india_only_ca_client's Brightlane Foods entry (an unrelated, pre-existing minimal stub with the exact same shape, { company, holding_pct } only) both only ever carried enough to prove the relationship, not a full transaction record. Filled in, prototypes/graph-pilot/profiles.js:

  • founder_indian_company: US foreign_corporations[0] gained tax_year_start: "2026-04-01" (the CFC's Indian-FY-aligned accounting year — pure Form 5471 metadata, not read by normalize.js/computation.js anywhere, so computationally inert). India unlisted_equity.transactions[0] gained acquisition_date: "2019-04-15", number_of_shares: 10000, cost_per_share: 10, cost_per_share_currency: "INR" — a plausible founder's original paid-up capital, well before the current tax year.
  • india_only_ca_client: Brightlane Foods' unlisted_equity.transactions[0] gained the same 4 fields, deliberately set to match the existing, separate share_buyback entry for the same company (original_acquisition_date: "2021-03-01", original_cost_inr: 250000) rather than inventing independent numbers — number_of_shares: 2500 × cost_per_share: 100 = exactly 250,000, so the two records agree on the same historical purchase instead of silently contradicting each other.

Deliberately left as still-HELD positions, not completed sales. Both entries got acquisition-side fields only — sale_date/sale_price_per_share stay unset. For Vikram this is a correctness requirement, not a style choice: the US side already shows him as an active 100%-owner CFC shareholder with ongoing GILTI income, so a sale on the India side would directly contradict that. For Kavya, the real taxable event (a buyback/tender) is already modeled separately via share_buyback; adding a second, independent sale to unlisted_equity would double up the same corporate action under two different schemas. engine/normalize.js explicitly gates all cost/date usage behind sale_date being present (if (!tx.sale_date || tx.sale_price_per_share == null) return; // still holding — no taxable event yet), so this was also verified computationally inert — no KPI tile, health score, or previously-documented total-tax figure moved.

A real, independent bug found and fixed along the way: layer1_india.html's addUnlistedRow() restored a saved transaction's shares/cost/sale fields from tx.shares_acquired/tx.cost_inr/tx.sale_value — field names syncUnlistedState() (the same file's own save path) never writes and engine/normalize.js never reads (both actually use number_of_shares/cost_per_share(+currency)/sale_price_per_share(+currency)). Meant a real round trip — advisor types values in, saves, reloads — silently lost every one of those fields, independent of anything to do with linked clients. Also added restore logic for 4 more fields the same function wrote but never read back at all: fmv_valuation_report_date, original_investment_currency, original_cost_in_foreign_currency, and the sale/cost currency selectors. Fixed to match what's actually written and actually computed.

Full 12-profile audit, same day: user asked for this same completeness check across every demo profile, not just the two touched above — done one profile at a time (not all at once) specifically to keep each change verifiable and avoid the risk of silently contradicting data across profiles. Read all 12 profiles (P1-P8, B1-B4) end to end, category by category (property, financial holdings, commodities, business income + asset blocks + MSME + partner firms, capital gains, other sources, deductions, foreign assets, US-side wages/K-1s/equity comp/retirement/real estate/FTC/itemized deductions/AMT), cross-checking against what each field's actual layer1_india.html/layer1_us.html card reads (not just what profiles.js sets) to catch the class of bug this whole entry started with — a has_X: true flag or a named entity whose card would still render blank/placeholder despite carrying real underlying data. Mechanically confirmed there were no other has_X: true + empty-array gaps anywhere in the file (grep swept for the pattern across all 12 profiles) and no other stray/meaningless null fields (every null found was a genuine, intentional value — presumptive_scheme: null = "regular books, not presumptive," addition_date: null = "no asset addition this year," payment_date: null = "MSME invoice not yet paid" — not a placeholder gap).

Result: 11 of 12 profiles were already fully complete — every populated category already carried full, realistic, internally-consistent data (verified individually: dual_resident_h1b, us_resident_indian_income, india_ror_us_income, us_citizen_expat_india, india_only_ca_client, us_only_cpa_client, india_pvt_ltd, foreign_holdco_poem_india, sharma_huf, greencard_retiree_india, plus founder_indian_company's own other categories beyond the entry already fixed above). Two profiles' US-side/India-side shells are deliberately, correctly minimal — india_only_ca_client (Kavya) and us_only_cpa_client (David) are explicit single-jurisdiction demo profiles built specifically to show WISING collapsing to a one-country view; fabricating data on their "absent" side would misrepresent the exact thing those profiles exist to demonstrate, so they were left untouched.

One more real gap found: us_ccorp_indian_sub's (Cloudspire) foreign_corporations entry used corp_name/country/ownership_pct instead of corporation_name/country_of_incorporation/ownership_percentage — the same short-name vs. real-form-name mismatch already described and aliased in engine/normalize.js's own comment (gap tracker US-26: "the real 'Add Foreign Corporation' UI... writes corporation_name/country_of_incorporation/ownership_percentage — none of which match corp_name/country/ownership_pct"). Verified both engine/normalize.js and the DAG's assets-nodes.js already alias both naming schemes identically (c.corp_name || c.corporation_name, etc. — the exact reason the comment gives for aliasing instead of renaming the form), so renaming the profile data to match what the real UI reads was confirmed computationally inert (targeted re-run of run-assets.js/run-entitytax.js showed identical output before and after) while fixing a card that previously rendered completely blank (Corporation Name/Country/Ownership% all empty) in layer1_us.html despite carrying real GILTI-relevant data underneath. Also added tax_year_start to this entry, same rationale as Vikram's.

Verified again after this pass: full regression suite clean (node tests/engine/run.js 79/79, all run-*.js harnesses, run-fuzz.js 3000 iterations/0 new divergences, npm run audit:dag, test-adapter.mjs 11,174 checks, test-shadow.mjs, clean npm run build from wiped generated dirs). Browser-confirmed via Playwright: loading us_ccorp_indian_sub and opening layer1_us.html now shows Corporation Name "Cloudspire India Pvt Ltd", Country "IN", Ownership 100, Tax Year Start "2026-04-01" — all previously blank.

Same-day follow-up: user reviewed Vikram's live Filings tab and found IRS Form 5471 (the CFC information return — exactly what his 100%-owned Nova Systems Pvt Ltd should require) genuinely missing from "Documents to File," and asked whether every demo profile's required-forms list had been checked this thoroughly, not just the field-completeness pass above. It hadn't — this was a different, more serious class of bug: not missing DATA, but a wrong TRIGGER CONDITION in buildDocuments() (engine/conflicts.js) / its DAG port (report-batch1-nodes.js). form_5471's condition read (model.assets.indianBusinesses || []).length > 0 && res.us.isResident — indianBusinesses is india.domestic_income.business_income.business_entries, the India-side "this taxpayer runs a PGBP business/profession" array, an entirely unrelated concept to Form 5471's actual legal trigger (a US person owning ≥10% of a foreign corporation, tracked separately as us.foreign_entities.owns_10_percent_foreign_corp/foreign_corporations[]). Found by noticing form_3ceb's own trigger, two lines below in the same function, already uses the correct signal (model.assets.usOwns10PctForeignCorp || (model.assets.usForeignCorps || []).length > 0) with a comment claiming form_5471 "already trusts" the same signal — which turned out to be aspirational, not actually true.

Fix, both engine/conflicts.js and the DAG's report-batch1-nodes.js (an explicit, user-approved exception to the frozen-engine rule — see H.8's precedent — since this is a correctness bug, not new data/logic, and leaving it wrong in engine mode would mean switching compute source silently changes filing-requirement correctness): form_5471 now checks the same CFC-ownership signal form_3ceb uses, usOwns10PctForeignCorp || usForeignCorps.length > 0. Also broadened the residency gate the same way form_1116 already handles it two lines above (res.us.isResident || model.entity.usReturnForm === "1120") — res.us.isResident alone is an individual-only concept (citizen/green-card/SPT) that would still miss a domestic C-corp CFC owner.

Impact traced against all 12 profiles before touching anything (a small Node script evaluating old vs. new trigger logic against every profile's actual data): 2 profiles correctly gain Form 5471 — founder_indian_company (Vikram, 100% Nova Systems owner — the reported bug) and us_ccorp_indian_sub (Cloudspire Inc, a domestic C-corp that owns its Indian subsidiary — previously missed by both the wrong array check and the individual-only residency gate). 3 profiles correctly lose a false positive Form 5471 that was firing for the wrong reason: dual_resident_h1b (Aarav has an unrelated India freelance business, owns_10_percent_foreign_corp: false), us_resident_indian_income (Rohan's small India consulting stake is explicitly modeled below the CFC threshold — his story text says this should trigger cfc_below_threshold, not a full Form 5471), and us_citizen_expat_india (Grace owns a foreign disregarded entity, not a foreign corporation — a real, different distinction Form 5471 doesn't cover). The other 7 profiles were correctly unaffected either way.

Verified: full regression suite re-run clean — node tests/engine/run.js (79/79), all run-*.js harnesses (every count identical to before, confirming engine and DAG stayed in lockstep after being fixed identically), run-fuzz.js (3000 iterations, 0 new divergences), npm run audit:dag, monitor-next/test-adapter.mjs (11,175 checks), monitor-next/test-shadow.mjs, and a clean npm run build from wiped generated dirs.
