You own ≥10% of an Indian company — potential Subpart F / GILTI inclusion," Documents-required count risen from 18 to 19 — screenshotted and visually confirmed, not just a DOM/text-content check.

**Same-day, second bug in the same family: user spotted IRS Form 1040-NR — Indian Pvt Ltd (company) — Required merged into Vikram's Filings tab and asked why a company would need an individual non-resident-alien return, and whether it was even for Vikram or the linked entity. It's for the linked entity (india_pvt_ltd/Nova Systems, merged in via H.11's owned-entities Filings merge — correctly tagged "Indian Pvt Ltd (company)," not Vikram himself). Root cause: india_pvt_ltd's US side is a deliberate "zero US exposure" placeholder shell (correct — the entity genuinely has none), but that shell fills final_us_residency_status with NON_RESIDENT_ALIEN as generic filler, and form_1040nr's trigger — model.treaty.files1040nr || (res.us.status === CONST.US_STATUS.NON_RESIDENT_ALIEN) — treated any** NRA status as an automatic 1040-NR filing requirement, completely overriding the entity's own explicit nra_specific.files_form_1040nr: false flag (Layer 1 US's own purpose-built "does this filer actually need to file 1040-NR" field — NRA status alone doesn't create a filing obligation; US-source income/a US trade-business does, which is exactly what that flag is meant to capture).

Fix, same file, same both-engine-and-DAG treatment as the Form 5471 fix above: dropped the redundant || status === NON_RESIDENT_ALIEN clause entirely — model.treaty.files1040nr (the explicit flag) is sufficient and correct on its own.

Impact checked against all 12 profiles before touching anything: 4 profiles correctly lose a false positive that was firing purely from NRA-status filler data, none of them actually needing a 1040-NR — india_only_ca_client (Kavya, whose entire profile purpose is demonstrating zero US filing obligations — this bug was directly undermining the one thing that profile exists to prove), india_pvt_ltd (the reported case), foreign_holdco_poem_india, and sharma_huf. india_ror_us_income (Anita, the one profile with a genuine 1040-NR obligation — real US-source rental/dividend/brokerage income, files_form_1040nr: true on her own data) correctly keeps it, confirming the flag alone is sufficient — it was never a false negative risk, only a false positive one. The other 7 profiles were unaffected either way.

A real debugging detour worth recording: initial browser verification kept showing "1040-NR" as present for all 4 supposedly-fixed profiles even after the code fix and a full resync — traced (via a temporary console.log inside the DAG node, confirming the fixed logic really was computing false) to a flaw in the verification script itself, not the fix: the Documents-to-File UI renders the entire catalog for every profile, showing each form's status as "Required" or "N/A" — a plain substring search for "1040-NR" matches the N/A row just as readily as a Required one. Rewritten to check the actual Required/N/A status text next to each form name, which confirmed the fix was correct the whole time.

Verified: full regression suite re-run clean again — node tests/engine/run.js (79/79), all run-*.js harnesses (identical counts), run-fuzz.js (3000 iterations, 0 new divergences), npm run audit:dag, test-adapter.mjs (11,175 checks), test-shadow.mjs, clean npm run build. Browser-confirmed via Playwright (status-aware check, not substring): india_only_ca_client/india_pvt_ltd/foreign_holdco_poem_india/sharma_huf all show Form 1040-NR as N/A; india_ror_us_income correctly still shows it Required; Form 5471 status re-confirmed unchanged from the fix above (Required for founder_indian_company/us_ccorp_indian_sub, N/A everywhere else) — no regression from touching the same function twice in one session.

H.13 — Full Filings-tab audit: all 31 (now 32) documents, Return Form logic, Compliance Calendar — 22 Jul 2026

After the Form 5471/1040-NR fixes above, the user asked a much bigger question directly: not "is this one form right for the 12 demo profiles," but is the whole Filings tab correct for any India/US Layer 1 input a real MVP user might enter, since the 12 demo profiles' own regression suite had stayed green through both bugs — neither was exposed by the specific values those profiles happen to carry. Scoped as a full audit of buildDocuments()'s all-31-entry CONST.DOCUMENTS catalog (engine/conflicts.js + the DAG's report-batch1-nodes.js), one real-legal-rule-vs-code comparison per form, done in batches (US information returns / US income-tax adjustment forms / US state+procedural / India forms), each batch verified with the full regression suite before the next.

New permanent artifact: prototypes/graph-pilot/run-documents-audit.js, joining the regular run-*.js suite. For every form audited, at least one synthetic scenario built to fire it and one built not to — using real base profiles (WISING.PROFILES) deep-cloned and mutated to isolate just the one fact pattern under test, independent of what the 12 named profiles happen to carry, checked against BOTH the real engine (WISING.analyze()) and the DAG (buildDocumentsResult via graph.resolve) in the same assertion — this catches an engine/DAG divergence and a wrong-legal-rule bug in one pass. This file is the durable answer to "how do you know it's covered": a coverage count that grows with each batch and re-runs on every future change, not a one-time manual read-through that can silently rot.

Batch A — US information returns (fincen_114/FBAR, form_8938/FATCA, form_5471/CFC, form_8621/PFIC, form_8865, form_3520) — 6 forms, 25 synthetic checks, all passing after fixes:

  • A systemic pattern across 4 triggers: res.us.isResident (individual-only — citizen/green-card/SPT) was the sole "is this a US person" gate, missing that a US-organized domestic entity (C-corp/S-corp/partnership/trust) can independently own foreign accounts/CFC stock/PFIC shares and carries its own filing obligation, separate from its owners'. Introduced a shared isUsPerson = res.us.isResident || isUsDomesticEntity (entity = usReturnForm in {1120, 1120-S, 1065, 1041}) and applied it to fincen_114, form_8938, form_5471 (broadened past the narrower "1120-only" fallback from the earlier same-day fix), and form_8621. Not applied to form_1116 — that one's C-corp-only "1120" fallback is correct as-is (Form 1118 vs 1116 is a genuine C-corp-specific distinction; a pass-through S-corp/partnership's FTC flows to its owners' own 1040/1116, not the entity's own return).
  • form_8621 (PFIC) was only checking India-side financial_holdings filtered for "mutual_fund" (model.assets.indianMutualFunds), completely ignoring model.assets.usPficHoldings — Layer 1 US's own dedicated "PFIC Holdings" card (us.foreign_entities.pfic_holdings, with its own has_pfics flag), defined in normalize.js/assets-nodes.js but never read by this trigger at all. A user who filled in only the US-side card (holds PFICs through a vehicle other than an India-side mutual fund line item) got Form 8621 silently marked N/A despite explicitly saying they hold PFICs. Masked in every demo profile because whoever built them always populated both fields together for the same holding. Fixed to check either signal; added a new usPficHoldingsRaw DAG leaf node (report-batch1-nodes.js) since none previously existed independently of the bundled assetsModelResult.
  • form_3520's gift/trust flags (receivedAbove100k, isTrustBeneficiary) fired unconditionally, with no US-person gate at all — Form 3520 (IRC §6039F) is a US-persons-only filing requirement; a pure NRA with zero US ties has no Form 3520 obligation regardless of what those flags say. Gated the whole trigger behind isUsPerson instead of just the pre-existing ppfInr/epfInr clause.
  • form_8865 (foreign partnership) confirmed as a genuine unmodeled-feature gap, not a trigger bug — no field anywhere in the schema (Layer 1 US or any of the 12 profiles) represents "owns an interest in a FOREIGN partnership" at all. Left honestly false with a clear comment, same convention as the existing GILTI-quantification gap, rather than half-wiring a trigger with nothing real underneath it to check.
  • A near-miss, corrected before shipping: form_nj1040 initially looked like an engine/DAG divergence (DAG's own report-batch1-nodes.js had a real trigger for it; the engine's conflicts.js had no trigger key at all, triggers["form_nj1040"] simply undefined). Investigated further before "fixing" it and found this is deliberate, not a bug — docs/GAP_TRACKER.md section H.7 already recorded that New Jersey state-tax support was added DAG-only (the frozen engine's computeUsStateTax/CONST.US_STATES genuinely has no NJ bracket data at all — new state coverage goes in the DAG per the standing frozen-engine policy), and H.7.3 explicitly documents form_nj1040 being stripped from documents[] comparisons across the whole harness suite for exactly this reason. A trigger was briefly added to the engine, then reverted once the H.7 entry was found — the shared constants.js CONST.DOCUMENTS catalog correctly has no form_nj1040 entry at all (the engine's own mirrored copy structurally omits the row, rather than including it with required: false), so adding a trigger for a catalog entry that doesn't exist on that side would have been dead code. run-documents-audit.js asserts this exact intended asymmetry (DAG: Required for an NJ resident; engine: the row is structurally absent) rather than treating it as something to fix.

Verified: full regression suite green after every change in this batch — node tests/engine/run.js (79/79), all run-*.js harnesses (identical counts to before, confirming zero impact on the 12 demo profiles — expected, since none of them combine a domestic non-C-corp entity with foreign PFIC/CFC/account facts, or an NRA with gift/trust flags set), run-fuzz.js (3000 iterations, 0 new divergences), npm run audit:dag, test-adapter.mjs (11,175 checks), test-shadow.mjs, clean npm run build from wiped generated dirs, and the new run-documents-audit.js itself (25/25).

Batch B — US income-tax adjustment forms (form_1116/FTC, form_2555/FEIE, form_8833/treaty position, form_1040nr, form_8960/NIIT, form_8959/Additional Medicare, form_6251/AMT) — 7 forms, 15 new synthetic checks (40 cumulative with Batch A), all passing:

  • form_8833 (treaty-based return position) had the same over-broad-signal problem as the Batch A fixes, just on the treaty side: it fired off model.treaty.files1040nr — true for any NRA filing a 1040-NR, regardless of whether that NRA is actually claiming any treaty benefit — rather than checking whether a treaty position is actually being taken. A plain NRA with US-source income taxed at ordinary statutory rates (no treaty claim at all) got Form 8833 marked Required with nothing to disclose. Fixed to use model.nra.treatyRateClaims (us.nra_specific.treaty_rate_claims, normalize.js:2475) — the precise "an actual treaty rate is being claimed" signal already relied on elsewhere in the same file for the W-8BEN-missing finding (conflicts.js:1038) — OR'd with the pre-existing res.dualResident/usTreatyResidence !== "none" clauses, which are genuine independent triggers (Article 4 tie-breaker, and DTAA residence election) left unchanged.
  • form_1116 (FTC) and form_2555 (FEIE) confirmed correct as-is — 1116's resident-or-C-corp gate was already reviewed and deliberately left alone in Batch A (see above); FEIE's claims_feie flag is a direct, unambiguous user election with no broader signal it should be checking instead.
  • form_1040nr re-verified correct after the earlier same-day fix (see the "IRS Form 1040-NR" bug above this section) — genuine NRA fires, resident alien does not.
  • form_8960 (NIIT) and form_6251 (AMT) confirmed correct — thresholds checked against real 2024/2025 IRS figures already encoded in CONST (NIIT_THRESHOLD: {single: 200000, mfj: 250000, mfs: 125000, hoh: 200000}, matching IRC §1411; AMT exemption/phase-out figures matching Form 6251 instructions), and both triggers correctly gate off the engine's own computed liability rather than a raw income figure, so no synthetic edge case around the threshold produced a wrong answer.
  • form_8959 (Additional Medicare Tax) confirmed correct, but exposed a test-construction lesson worth recording: the trigger reads computed.usTax.additionalMedicareUsd, a pure pass-through of us.withholding_and_estimated.additional_medicare_tax_owed_usd (normalize.js:2723) — this figure is computed client-side by layer1_us.html itself (Math.max(0, (totalMedicareWages - threshold) * 0.009), lines ~9104-9109) and stored as an already-computed result, the same architectural pattern as files_form_1040nr. The frozen engine/DAG never derives it from raw W-2 wages. A first draft of the synthetic test set only raw wages_w2 data and failed (expected true, engine=false, dag=false) — not a product bug, but the test not simulating what a real filled-out form would have pre-computed and stored. Fixed by setting additional_medicare_tax_owed_usd directly in the synthetic profile, matching what the real form's client-side JS would produce for that wage level.

Verified: full regression suite green after the form_8833 fix — node tests/engine/run.js (79/79), all run-*.js harnesses (unchanged counts, confirming zero impact on the 12 demo profiles), run-fuzz.js (3000 iterations, 0 new divergences), npm run audit:dag, test-adapter.mjs (11,175 checks), test-shadow.mjs, clean npm run build from wiped generated dirs, and run-documents-audit.js (40/40, Batch A+B cumulative).

Batch C — US state + procedural forms (form_540/CA, form_it201/NY, form_nj1040/NJ, form_8802, form_4868, form_8288/FIRPTA) — 6 forms, 14 new synthetic checks (54 cumulative with A+B), all triggers confirmed correct as written, no code changes needed:

  • form_540/form_it201 (state resident returns): computeUsStateTax (computation.js:1133) already has two deliberate, documented scope limits that this batch verified hold in practice: it returns null for any non-individual entity kind (business entities file a separate franchise/entity-level state return, an unrelated unmodeled regime) and null for any NRA (Form 1040-NR filer — no state-source-income split is modeled, only full-year-resident worldwide income via federal AGI). Synthetic tests confirmed both: a domestic C-corp with CA facts on file does not get a personal Form 540, and an NRA with CA facts on file does not either — both real, pre-existing gaps (not this batch's bugs) already called out in the function's own header comment, left as-is rather than half-wired.
  • form_nj1040 already covered by Batch A's synthetic test (the deliberate DAG-only asymmetry per H.7) — no new test needed here.
  • form_8802 (US residency certification / Form 6166) confirmed correct — deliberately mirrors the India-side trc trigger exactly (same condition: dual resident, or a treaty-residence position claimed on either side), since Form 6166 (obtained via Form 8802) IS the document India's TRC requirement expects the US to issue. Verified against both a dual-resident and a genuine treaty-claiming NRA (both Required) and a domestic C-corp with no treaty facts on file (N/A).
  • form_4868 (extension) confirmed correct — fires for any taxpayer with US scope at all, by design (see the pre-existing comment: WISING can't know in advance who will actually end up filing late, so every US filer is informed of the form's existence ahead of the deadline). Verified a pure India-only profile with zero US scope correctly gets N/A.
  • form_8288 (FIRPTA withholding) confirmed correct for the case it models — fires only when nra_specific.us_real_property_disposed is true AND actual withholding was collected (firpta_withholding_usd > 0), correctly staying N/A when no disposition occurred or when a Form 8288-B certificate reduced withholding to $0 (a genuine exemption, not a missed filing). Confirmed scope gap, same class as Form 8865 in Batch A: FIRPTA (IRC §897/1445) applies to any "foreign person" disposing of a US real property interest, including a foreign corporation — but nra_specific is only populated for individual NRA filers; there is no schema field anywhere for a foreign entity's US real property disposition. Left unmodeled with no trigger change, same convention as Form 8865: a real gap to note for a future Layer 1 field, not something to half-wire against nothing.

Verified: node tests/engine/run.js (79/79) and run-documents-audit.js (54/54, Batch A+B+C cumulative) after adding this batch's tests — no engine or DAG code changed in this batch, so the rest of the harness suite (run-*.js, run-fuzz.js, audit:dag, test-adapter.mjs, test-shadow.mjs, build) carries over unchanged from Batch B's already-green run.

Batch D — India forms (form_67/Form 44, trc, form_10f, schedule_fa, schedule_fsi_tr, form_15ca_cb, schedule_al, form_3cb_3cd, form_3ceb, form_26as_ais_tis, form_16_16a, lrs_form_a2) — 12 forms, 29 new synthetic checks (83 cumulative), the two biggest bugs of the whole audit, both fixed in this batch:

  • form_67 (India FTC / Form 44) — the most consequential bug found in the entire audit, present since the very first commit. The trigger was model.income.us.foreignSourceTotal.usd > 0 || model.taxesPaid.us.total.usd > 0 || res.india.isResident — two problems compounding: (1) foreignSourceTotal is the wrong field entirely — it's the FOREIGN-source income on the US model's own view (India-source income reported on a US 1040, feeding the US's own Form 1116 FTC computation), not "US-source income taxed in India," which is what Form 67 actually requires and what this trigger's own why text says ("Foreign (US) income is being offered to tax in India"); the correct field, usSourceTotal, was sitting one line below in the sibling schedule_fsi_tr trigger the whole time. (2) res.india.isResident was OR'd in as an unconditional catch-all — true for BOTH ROR and RNOR — making the whole expression evaluate to true for essentially any India resident/RNOR regardless of whether they had any US income or paid any US tax at all. Verified against all 12 demo profiles: 6 of 12 were false positives before this fix — india_only_ca_client, india_pvt_ltd, foreign_holdco_poem_india, sharma_huf (zero US income/tax on file, so nothing to claim FTC on) and, worse, us_resident_indian_income and founder_indian_company (both outright India-NR, not even resident — a true non-resident reports only India-source income on their return, so foreign-income relief is categorically inapplicable). Fixed to res.india.status === ROR && (usSourceTotal.usd > 0 || taxesPaid.us.total.usd > 0) — ROR-only (not RNOR either, matching the schedule_fa gate already 2 lines below, since RNOR isn't taxed on foreign income in India any more than NR is) properly ANDed with the real income/tax signal instead of OR'd as a catch-all.
  • schedule_fsi_tr — the same bug class, same root cause, on the very next line. Form 67's own why text says "Schedule FSI/TR must accompany the ITR" — the two are filed together for the identical fact pattern — yet schedule_fsi_tr's trigger had no residency gate at all (taxesPaid.us.total.usd > 0 || usSourceTotal.usd > 0, unconditionally). Confirmed false positive on the 3 genuinely-NR demo profiles (us_resident_indian_income, founder_indian_company, us_only_cpa_client) before the fix. Fixed with the identical ROR gate as form_67.
  • schedule_fa (Foreign Assets) — a real coverage gap, not a wrong-field bug. model.accounts.accounts (used for the "holds a non-India account" check) is built by aggregateAccounts() from bank_accounts ONLY — it never included model.assets.usSecurities (us.financial_holdings: foreign brokerage/securities holdings, separately FBAR/FATCA-reportable per Layer 1 US's own is_fbar_reportable flag on each entry, and already correctly rolled into the FBAR/FATCA aggregate-peak-dollar figure via the client-computed fbar_aggregate_peak_usd pass-through — so FBAR/FATCA's own dollar thresholds were never wrong, only Schedule FA's own separate per-account list check). A synthetic ROR holding a $500k US brokerage account and nothing else (no bank account, no US-source income) showed Schedule FA as not_triggered before this fix — a real, serious miss given Black Money Act penalty exposure (up to 120% of undisclosed asset value) is independent of whether any tax is actually due. Fixed by adding a check against usSecurities entries with a nonzero peak_balance_usd. Related, out-of-scope gap noted for a future audit: the schedule_fa_inconsistent finding and the XB-7 Black Money Act exposure quantification (both in the Conflicts/Monitor tabs, not the Filings tab) independently reimplement the exact same accounts+usSourceTotal-only check and have the identical gap — not touched here since this pass was explicitly scoped to the Filings tab.
  • trc/form_10f, form_15ca_cb/lrs_form_a2, schedule_al, form_3cb_3cd, form_3ceb, form_26as_ais_tis/form_16_16a all confirmed correct against their real legal triggers (Rule 21AB self-declaration, Rule 37BB outward-remittance certification, the ₹50L Schedule AL threshold with company/firm correctly excluded — and, on the DAG side only, AOP/Trust too, per the already-documented H.6 entity-routing divergence — since those file their own unconditional balance-sheet requirement instead, s.44AB tax-audit turnover thresholds with the 95%-digital-receipts test, s.92E transfer-pricing certification reusing the same CFC-ownership signal as Form 5471, and the two unconditional India-scope reconciliation forms).

Verified: full regression suite green after all fixes in this batch — node tests/engine/run.js (79/79), all run-*.js harnesses (unchanged counts on the 12 demo profiles except the 3 documents whose status genuinely changed, form_67/schedule_fsi_tr/schedule_fa — confirmed both engine and DAG agree exactly across all 12 profiles for all 3), run-fuzz.js (3000 iterations, 0 new divergences), npm run audit:dag, test-adapter.mjs (11,175 checks), test-shadow.mjs, clean npm run build from wiped generated dirs, run-documents-audit.js (83/83, Batch A+B+C+D cumulative), and a live browser check on the us_resident_indian_income (NR) demo profile confirming Form 44 and Schedule FSI/TR now correctly render "N/A" instead of "Required" on the actual Filings tab.

Batch E — catalog completeness, Return Form logic, and Compliance Calendar dates (the three other things a real Filings tab shows besides the Documents list) — 2 real gaps found and fixed, one confirmed-correct area, 4 new synthetic checks (87 cumulative):

  • Catalog completeness — Form 8858 (Foreign Disregarded Entities) confirmed missing, added DAG-only. Reg. §1.6038-2 requires Form 8858 from a US person who owns a foreign disregarded entity or foreign branch — the 31-entry CONST.DOCUMENTS catalog had no row for it at all, despite Layer 1 US collecting the exact signal (a Schedule C row with llc_type: "foreign_disregarded" — the SAME field normalize.js already reads to route income into foreignSelfEmployment, plus a direct foreign_entities.owns_foreign_disregarded_entity flag). Confirmed via us_citizen_expat_india (Grace Thomas), who has a real India-based consulting sole proprietorship on file marked llc_type: "foreign_disregarded" and got no Form 8858 prompt whatsoever. This is a brand-new document, not a fix to an existing trigger, so per the standing frozen-engine policy it's added DAG-only (report-batch1-nodes.js's own DOCUMENTS_CATALOG, same mechanism as form_nj1040 — the engine's CONST.DOCUMENTS has no matching entry, so the row is structurally absent on that side, not a divergence to fix). Every harness that compares documents[]/calendar docIds byte-for-byte between engine and DAG needed the same one-line addition to its existing form_nj1040 strip-list (run-analyze.js, run-report1.js, run-monitor.js, run-fuzz.js, test-adapter.mjs, lib/shadow-core.js) — plus a small correction in test-adapter.mjs/shadow-core.js: summary.requiredDocs (a DAG-computed count baked in from the un-stripped documents list) needed the same adjustment, since test-adapter.mjs's own ±2 numeric tolerance was silently swallowing the resulting 1-off difference rather than genuinely agreeing — fixed to decrement the count precisely instead of relying on tolerance to hide a known gap. Two other already-known, real, unmodeled catalog gaps (Form 8865 foreign-partnership ownership, Form 8288 foreign-entity FIRPTA — both from Batches A/C, no Layer 1 field exists for either) were reconfirmed still open and are not fixed here, since (unlike Form 8858) there's no real underlying field to key a trigger on.
  • Compliance Calendar — a second real bug, same "audit-case" family as form_3cb_3cd/schedule_al: the India ITR filing deadline was missing its s.92E tier entirely. monitoring.js's calendar builder had exactly two tiers for the India filing deadline (31 Jul non-audit / 31 Oct audit case), but s.139(1) Explanation 2(a)(ii) gives an assessee with a s.92E transfer-pricing reporting obligation (the SAME signal form_3ceb's own trigger already uses — a real cross-border AE ownership relationship) a due date of 30 Nov, one month past the plain audit-case date, independent of whether they're also an audit case for a separate reason (e.g. being a company). Verified against the demo profiles: us_ccorp_indian_sub and founder_indian_company (both have real CFC ownership, so a genuine Form 3CEB obligation) were showing 31 Oct as "the" deadline before this fix — one month earlier than their real statutory due date, which could cause premature/rushed filing or wrong-basis interest exposure if that date were relied on for planning. Fixed in both engine/monitoring.js and the DAG's report-batch6-nodes.js (a genuine correctness bug in existing frozen-engine date logic, not a new feature — H.8-class exception, fixed on both sides) by adding the 30 Nov tier, checked ahead of the existing audit-case tier since s.92E always extends beyond it when both apply. Live-verified in the browser: founder_indian_company's Filings tab now shows "ITR + Form 44 (s.92E/transfer-pricing case) — Nov 30, 2027" instead of the old 31 Oct date.
  • Return Form logic (India ITR-1 through ITR-7 selection, US 1040/1040-NR/1120/1120-S/1065/1041 selection) reviewed and confirmed correct. computeIndiaItrForm (computation.js:1687) already independently re-derives the correct ITR form from real facts (total income, capital-gains composition under the AY 2026-27 s.198-only ITR-1/4 tolerance, foreign income/assets, crypto, house-property count, agricultural income, lottery/gaming income, brought-forward losses, speculative/F&O activity, presumptive-scheme purity) and cross-checks it against Layer 1 India's own self-reported recommendation, surfacing a disagreement as both an inline trace note AND a real india_itr_form_mismatch finding rather than silently picking one — already a robust, well-designed cross-check, not something this pass needed to change. The US side (E.usReturnForm, normalize.js:2346) is a simpler, already-reviewed-in-Batch-A 6-way mapping (1120/1120-S/1065/1041 by entity type, else 1040-NR/1040 by the explicit files_form_1040nr flag) with no further gaps found.

Verified: full regression suite green after both fixes — node tests/engine/run.js (79/79), all run-*.js harnesses (unchanged counts on the 12 demo profiles except the India-filing-deadline date on the 2 profiles with a genuine s.92E obligation), run-fuzz.js (3000 iterations, 0 new divergences), npm run audit:dag, test-adapter.mjs (11,175 checks), test-shadow.mjs (including the summary.requiredDocs precision fix), clean npm run build from wiped generated dirs, run-documents-audit.js (87/87, Batch A+B+C+D+E cumulative), and live browser verification of both the Form 8858 row (Grace Thomas) and the 30 Nov calendar date (founder_indian_company).

H.13 coverage summary — how this audit knows it covered everything

The user's original question was specific: "how will I know that you have covered everything and have completely checked each and everything for the filings tab specifically." The concrete, durable answer:

  1. Every one of the 31 original CONST.DOCUMENTS entries was individually reviewed against its real legal trigger (a specific IRC section, Treasury regulation, or Indian Income-tax Act/Rule citation for each — named inline in the GAP_TRACKER writeup above for every batch), not just spot-checked against the 12 demo profiles' existing values. Every review is attributed to a specific batch (A: 6 US information returns; B: 7 US income-tax adjustment forms; C: 6 US state+procedural forms; D: 12 India forms) above.
  2. 6 real bugs were found and fixed this way — 2 were pure false positives severe enough to affect a majority of demo profiles (form_67/Form 44 was wrong on 6 of 12; schedule_fsi_tr on 3 of 12), and would never have been caught by the existing regression suite alone, since that suite only ever re-checks the SAME 12 fixed profiles' SAME fixed values. A 32nd document (Form 8858) was found entirely missing from the catalog and added.
  3. prototypes/graph-pilot/run-documents-audit.js is the durable, re-runnable answer, not a one-time manual pass: 87 synthetic test cases, each built by taking a real base profile and mutating just the one fact pattern under test, checked against BOTH the engine and the DAG in the same assertion. It joins the permanent run-*.js regression suite — every future change to buildDocuments()/buildDocumentsResult or the Compliance Calendar re-runs all 87 cases automatically, so a future regression on any of these 6 fixed bugs (or the correctly-scoped triggers around them) would be caught immediately rather than requiring another manual audit.
  4. Confirmed, still-open gaps were separated from fixed bugs and named explicitly, not left ambiguous: Form 8865 (foreign partnership) and Form 8288 for a foreign entity's (as opposed to individual NRA's) US real property disposition are real, honestly-unmodeled gaps — no Layer 1 field represents either fact pattern at all, so there's nothing to wire a trigger to. The parallel schedule_fa_inconsistent/XB-7 gap in the Conflicts tab (same missing-usSecurities-signal bug as schedule_fa, just in a different tab) was identified but deliberately left out of scope, since this pass was scoped to the Filings tab specifically.
  5. Return Form logic and Compliance Calendar dates — the two other real things the Filings tab shows besides the Documents list — got the same treatment, not just the Documents list: Return Form selection was reviewed and confirmed already correct (with its own built-in cross-check against Layer 1's self-reported recommendation); the Compliance Calendar's date logic was reviewed line-by-line against real statutory due dates and had its own genuine bug (the missing s.92E tier) found and fixed the same way as everything else.

Genuinely out of scope (not part of "the Filings tab" — separate, future work if wanted): the Conflicts tab's schedule_fa_inconsistent/XB-7 gap noted above, and the two structural unmodeled-feature gaps (Form 8865, entity-level FIRPTA).

H.14 — Catalog completeness, round 2: external-research pass — 22 Jul 2026

Follow-on to H.13. The user's own follow-up question was pointed: "how do I know that you have comprehensively covered every form that might be required... in both India and US?" The honest answer at that point was that H.13's method (noticing unused Layer 1 fields that pointed at a missing form, e.g. Form 8858) has a hard blind spot — it can only find a gap where the data to trigger a form already exists somewhere in Layer 1. It can never find a gap where Layer 1 never collects the underlying fact at all. Closing that blind spot needs a genuinely different method: pull a canonical checklist from authoritative external sources (not just re-reading this codebase) and diff it against the 32-entry catalog. Scoped, per the user's explicit instruction, to a reasonably thorough pass — not an exhaustive enumeration of every IRS/India form that has ever existed.

Method: web research against IRS.gov, IRS form instructions, and established cross-border/expat-tax practitioner checklists (US side); incometax.gov.in guidance and NRI/foreign-income tax-preparer checklists (India side). Every candidate form found this way was checked against Layer 1's actual field list and the engine's actual computed output — same "is there a real signal, or nothing to hook a trigger to" test as H.13 — before deciding whether to add it, note it as an honest gap, or rule it out as out-of-scope for a cross-border-specific tool.

3 more real, well-supported forms found missing and added (all DAG-only, same form_nj1040/form_8858 pattern — brand-new documents, not existing-trigger fixes, per the standing frozen-engine policy):

  • Form 3520-A (Annual Information Return of Foreign Trust) — the trust's OWN annual return (filed by the trust or its US agent), distinct from Form 3520 (the US owner/beneficiary's own return, already modeled). A US person treated as the OWNER of a foreign trust under the grantor-trust rules — the same "Indian PPF/EPF may be treated as trusts" fact form_3520's own catalog description already calls out — triggers this too. Added using only the OWNERSHIP subset of form_3520's existing condition (ppfInr > 0 || epfInr > 0), deliberately excluding the gift-received/beneficiary-distribution subset, since neither of those makes the US person the trust's "owner."
  • Form 29B (MAT Report) — a CA-certified report of book profit under s.115JB, required whenever Minimum Alternate Tax actually applies. The frozen engine already computes this exact signal (computed.indiaTax.matApplied, computeIndiaEntityTax/entitytax-nodes.js) — it was just never promoted to a Filings-tab document, the same "computed elsewhere, never surfaced as a filing requirement" pattern form_6251/AMT had before Batch B. Gated on indiaIsCompany (MAT only applies to companies u/s 115JB; AOP/trust/firm branches of the same computation hardcode matApplied: false, correctly, since MAT doesn't apply to them).
  • Form 10-IEA (Old Regime Election) — the declaration an individual/HUF with business/professional income must file to opt out of the default new regime (s.115BAC) or switch back, vs. a salaried/other-income-only filer who can just tick a box on the ITR itself. Found this exact condition ALREADY independently derived elsewhere in the codebase — a diagnostic-only "form_10iea" entry in checks-registry-nodes.js — never promoted to a real Filings-tab document. Reused that exact condition (taxRegime === "OLD" && !company && !firm && !aop && !trust && businessComputation.businessInr > 0) rather than re-deriving it.

Confirmed candidates NOT added, with the reason for each:

  • Form 926 (transfer of property to a foreign corporation) and Form 5472 (25%-foreign-owned US corp/foreign corp engaged in US trade or business) — both real US international-information-return forms, but no Layer 1 field represents either fact pattern (a capital contribution TO a foreign corp; a foreign person owning ≥25% of a US entity) at all. Same honest-gap category as Form 8865/entity-level FIRPTA from H.13 — nothing to wire a trigger to, not half-wired against a guess.
  • Form 8990 (§163(j) business interest expense limitation) — a real, well-documented US form, but a general large-business threshold rule ($32M+ average gross receipts, or partnership/S-corp interest allocation) with no cross-border-specific character at all — ruled out as outside this tool's cross-border-specific mission, same reasoning the product already applies elsewhere (e.g. not modeling every domestic AMT preference item beyond ISO/SALT).

Verified: full regression suite green — node tests/engine/run.js (79/79), all run-*.js harnesses, run-fuzz.js (3000 iterations, 0 new divergences), npm run audit:dag, test-adapter.mjs (11,175 checks), test-shadow.mjs, clean npm run build from wiped generated dirs, run-documents-audit.js (94/94, Batch A-F cumulative), and live browser verification of all 3 new documents (Form 3520-A, Form 29B, Form 10-IEA) rendering correctly on the Filings tab. One harness-precision fix needed along the way: summary.requiredDocs (a DAG-computed count baked in from the un-stripped documents list) needed the same per-DAG-only-document adjustment in run-fuzz.js/run-analyze.js that Batch E had already applied to test-adapter.mjs/shadow-core.js — without it, run-fuzz.js's random profile generator surfaced real (if masked) count mismatches once a THIRD DAG-only document existed with a common-enough trigger condition (Form 10-IEA) to actually appear "required" in a 3000-iteration run; Form 8858 alone (a much rarer fact pattern) never had enough random-generation exposure to surface the same latent gap in Batch E.

Now 35 total documents in the catalog (21 US + 14 India), up from 32 after H.13's Form 8858 addition and 31 originally.

Still genuinely out of scope: everything named as out-of-scope at the end of H.13 (the Conflicts-tab schedule_fa_inconsistent/XB-7 gap, Form 8865, entity-level FIRPTA), plus Form 926/5472/8990 named above.

H.14.1 — follow-up, same day: Form 10-IC / Form 10-ID (the COMPANY-side equivalents of Form 10-IEA). User question after reading H.14's Form 10-IEA writeup: "This is for the company?" — correctly probing an ambiguity in how the entry was described. Answer: no, form_10iea's trigger explicitly excludes companies (!indiaIsCompany && !indiaIsFirm && !indiaIsAop && !indiaIsTrust) — it's the individual/HUF mechanism only. Companies use two entirely different, mutually exclusive forms: Form 10-IC (electing the s.115BAA 22% concessional rate) and Form 10-ID (electing the s.115BAB 15% new-manufacturing rate). Added both, DAG-only, same pattern as every other H.13/H.14 addition — reusing indiaOpt115baa/indiaOpt115bab, the exact flags entitytax-nodes.js already reads to compute the entity's actual tax rate (the india_pvt_ltd demo profile already has opt_115baa: true on file, so Form 10-IC now correctly shows Required for it — confirmed live in the browser, nested under its owner "Founder · Indian company" in the Clients tab). 7 new synthetic checks added to run-documents-audit.js (101 cumulative). Same full regression suite re-run clean (run-fuzz.js 0 divergences, test-adapter.mjs 11,175 checks, test-shadow.mjs, clean build). 37 total documents in the catalog now (21 US + 16 India).

H.14.2 — follow-up, same day: two more "is this exhaustive" examples checked, one real gap named and then scoped out by explicit product decision. User pushed back on two illustrative "still might be missing" examples from H.14's own closing caveat, asking why they hadn't actually been run down. Checked both for real, not rhetorically:

  • "US person as beneficial owner of a foreign estate" — not actually a gap; the example was wrong. Form 3520 (already in the catalog, IRC §6039F) covers both gifts from foreign individuals AND bequests from foreign estates under the identical $100k threshold/mechanism — there's no separate ongoing-filer form for estates the way trusts have 3520-A (an estate gets administered and closed, not an ongoing entity). Layer 1's actual field ("Received Foreign Gifts (Any Amount)?") is worded broadly enough to already capture a bequest.
  • "Transfer-pricing safe-harbour election" — a real, confirmed gap: Form 3CEFA, the application a taxpayer files to opt into India's Safe Harbour Rules (Rule 10E, s.92CB) — genuinely distinct from Form 3CEB (already in the catalog, which just certifies whatever international transactions happened, election or not). No Layer 1 field exists for "opted into safe harbour." Explicit product decision (user, 22 Jul 2026): not adding it — this tool isn't going deeper into transfer-pricing-specific elections beyond the existing Form 3CEB certification, so this is scoped out deliberately, not left as a pending TODO. Filed alongside Form 8990 (H.14's other "real but outside this tool's mission" exclusion) rather than the Form 8865/926/5472-style "real gap, no data, would add if the data existed" category — the distinction being those three would be added the moment a Layer 1 field existed, while Form 3CEFA (like 8990) is out of scope regardless of what data exists, by product-scope decision.

Maintenance

  • Re-verify all "verified current" claims after: every Union Budget (Feb), every Finance Act notification (Mar), US filing-season changes (Jan), and any OBBBA technical corrections.
  • When an item ships, move it to the relevant "verified current / already modeled" list with the commit hash rather than deleting the row.
  • When a 🟡 row gets its Layer 1 field (prompt sent, file returned, engine wired), flip it to 🟢-done and note the commit, same as any other status change.

WISING — Developer Handoff & Integration Guide

This is the single entry point for a developer taking the prototype to production. It explains **what the code is, how it runs, the data contract, and exactly where to plug in a real backend.**

TL;DR: The tax engine is plain, framework-free JavaScript and is the crown
jewel. It already runs in Node with zero changes. The dashboard is a **Next.js
(React) app**. "Integrating properly" mostly means: move the engine server-side
behind an API, replace localStorage with a real datastore, and add
auth/persistence. The engine itself does not need rewriting to do that.

1. Stack

PartTech
Dashboard (monitor-next/)Next.js 14 (App Router) · React 18 · JavaScript/JSX (no TS) · Tailwind CSS · react-simple-maps
Tax engine (engine/)Vanilla JavaScript (ES5-style IIFEs attaching to a global WISING). No framework, no build step. Runs in a browser <script> and in Node via require().
Layer 1 intake formsStandalone HTML + vanilla JS + Tailwind (layer1_india.html, layer1_us.html, router.html)
Persistence (today)localStorage only. No backend, no database.

index.html in a build is compiled output, not source — Next.js emits it plus _next/static/chunks/*.js. Edit the .jsx source, not the HTML.

2. Repo map

engine/                     ← THE ENGINE (source of truth, pure JS, portable)
  constants.js              ← FX, thresholds, tax tables, storage-key contract
  normalize.js              ← Layer 1 JSON → normalized model (the input schema)
  computation.js            ← India + US tax, FTC, FEIE gate, cross-basis
  conflicts.js              ← WISING.analyze() entry point; findings/documents/FTC
  monitoring.js             ← residency day-counters, calendar, health, alerts
  profiles.js               ← demo test taxpayers (WISING.PROFILES)
  sample-data.js            ← the default demo taxpayer (WISING.SAMPLE)

layer1_india.html           ← India intake (writes localStorage 'wising_layer1_india_state')
layer1_us.html              ← US intake    (writes 'wising_us_state')
router.html                 ← profile router (writes 'wising_router_state')

monitor-next/               ← the dashboard (Next.js)
  app/page.jsx              ← shell, state, view switching
  components/               ← Sidebar, Header, KpiCards, DetailTable, WorldMap,
                              UsStatesMap, Views.jsx (all tab panels)
  lib/wising.js             ← bridges the engine to React (analyze/snapshot/profiles)
  lib/logic.js              ← status classification, KPI rollups, palette
  lib/engine/               ← GENERATED copy of /engine (do not edit; see §7)
  scripts/sync-engine.js    ← copies /engine → lib/engine on predev/prebuild
  next.config.mjs           ← static-export config (see §6)

docs/                       ← this file + COVERAGE_AND_ARCHITECTURE.md

3. Run it

cd monitor-next
npm install
npm run dev        # http://localhost:3000  (live dev server)
# or
npm run build      # static export → monitor-next/out/  (open out/index.html offline)

predev/prebuild run scripts/sync-engine.js, which copies /engine/*.js into monitor-next/lib/engine/ so the app and the root engine never drift.

The engine also runs standalone in Node (this is how the logic is unit-tested):

["constants","normalize","computation","monitoring","conflicts","profiles","sample-data"]
  .forEach(f => require("./engine/" + f + ".js"));
const W = globalThis.WISING;
const result = W.analyze({ india: W.SAMPLE.india, us: W.SAMPLE.us, router: W.SAMPLE.router });

4. Data flow

Layer 1 forms ──write──▶ localStorage ──read──▶ WISING.normalize() ──▶ WISING.compute()
  (India/US JSON)         (3 keys)               (model)               (tax/FTC/cross-basis)
                                                                            │
        dashboard ◀── React (lib/wising.js) ◀── WISING.analyze() ◀─────────┘
                                                 (findings, documents, monitoring…)
  • WISING.analyze(opts) is the single engine entry point.

opts = { router, india, us } — pass the three Layer 1 JSON blobs directly (used for tests/profiles), or omit them and the engine reads localStorage.

  • It returns: `{ model, computed, findings, documents, ftcReport, taxComputation,

reconciliation, monitoring, summary }`.

  • The input schema = whatever normalize.js reads (safe(...) paths). Treat

normalize.js as the contract spec between Layer 1 and the engine.

localStorage contract (engine/constants.js › STORAGE_KEYS)

KeyWritten byHolds
wising_router_staterouter.htmltaxpayer profile (dual/single, base year, US flags)
wising_layer1_india_statelayer1_india.htmlIndia intake JSON
wising_us_statelayer1_us.htmlUS intake JSON
wising_active_profiledashboardwhich demo profile is loaded (demo only)

5. Integration checklist (taking it to production)

  1. Move the engine server-side. engine/*.js already runs in Node unchanged.

Wrap WISING.analyze() in an API endpoint (POST /analyze with the three blobs → returns the result object). Keeps the tax logic off the client.

  1. Replace localStorage with a datastore + API. The Layer 1 forms currently

persist to localStorage; point them at your API instead. The engine doesn't care where the JSON comes from — it just needs the same shapes normalize.js expects.

  1. Wire the real Layer 1 forms (they already emit the correct shapes) or, better,

rebuild them as React components inside the app and POST to the API.

  1. Auth / multi-tenant / persistence — none exists yet. Add before real client data.
  2. Recommended: migrate engine/ to TypeScript. This is a tax engine; typing the

model (and INR vs USD money objects) will catch a real class of bugs. It can be done incrementally (engine first, then the React app).

  1. Drop the static-export hacks once served by a real server (see §6).

6. Static-export notes (why index.html works offline)

next.config.mjs uses output: "export", assetPrefix: ".", images.unoptimized. The relative assetPrefix makes the bundle work from file:// (double-click). When you serve the app from a real server/CDN, remove assetPrefix: "." and (usually) output: "export" and serve Next normally. The Layer 1 HTML files are copied into monitor-next/public/ so the export can link to them.

7. Gotchas

  • Never edit monitor-next/lib/engine/ — it is generated from /engine by

sync-engine.js. Edit /engine/*.js; the copy is refreshed on npm run dev/build. (It is committed so the app builds without the root; treat it as build output.)

  • The engine files are side-effect IIFEs that attach to a global. **Import order

matters**: constants → normalize → computation → monitoring → conflicts → profiles.

  • If the dev server serves stale chunks after an engine change, kill lingering

next-server/next start processes and rebuild.

  • FX is a flat 83.0 INR/USD (constants.js › FX). Production needs per-date rates.

8. Demo vs production data

  • engine/sample-data.js (WISING.SAMPLE) is the default demo taxpayer;

engine/profiles.js (WISING.PROFILES) are the one-click test taxpayers. Both are fixtures for the demo — replace with real intake in production.

  • Some cross-basis / computation rows are planning-grade (flagged estimate),

because Layer 1 doesn't yet capture every line-item. To make them filing-exact, collect and wire:

  • Per-transaction FX (Rule 115 / SBI TTBR) instead of the flat rate.
  • US rental depreciation basis (currently modeled) — needs the property's

depreciable cost basis.

  • Capital-gains cost basis + acquisition dates for US holding-period /

USD-basis recomputation.

  • FEIE foreign-housing exclusion (only the base FEIE amount is modeled).
  • Tax tables in constants.js are TY2025/FY2025-26; they need annual maintenance.

9. Where the value is

engine/ is deliberately decoupled from the UI. A developer can lift the whole folder into any backend (Node/serverless) and get the full India⇄US computation, FTC/cross-basis reconciliation, FEIE gate, PFIC/CFC/FBAR/LRS detection, residency day-counting and the compliance calendar — without touching React. Start there.

US Layer 1 → Monitor — Compliance Coverage Audit

Step-by-step audit of the US Layer 1 intake against what the Monitor currently surfaces. For each compliance area: what Layer 1 collects, the **coverage status, where it shows in the Monitor today, and if not covered, where it will land** (which tab / engine module).

Legend — ✅ Covered · 🟡 Partial (read but simplified / display-only) · ❌ Not yet

Monitor surfaces referenced: Monitor (map + KPIs + Conflicts), Residency, Holdings (income & assets), Filings (calendar + FTC + tax computation + cross-basis), Documents (form checklist), Accounts (reporting limits + foreign accounts). Engine modules: normalize.js (intake→model), computation.js (tax), conflicts.js (findings/documents/FTC), monitoring.js (residency/calendar).

1. Residency & Status determination

Layer 1 stepStatusWhere in Monitor / where it will come
Tax entity type (individual / entity)✅model.entity; drives which computation runs. Residency + Filings.
LLC IRS classification election❌Engine reads entity kind but not the check-the-box election. → normalize entity block + a Filings note.
Filing status (single/MFJ/MFS/HOH) + NRA restriction🟡Used for brackets/std deduction/NIIT thresholds. The NRA-can't-MFJ restriction + §6013(h)/(g) elections are not modelled. → computation + a Conflicts check.
Community property (MFS)❌→ computation (income splitting) + Conflicts.
Taxpayer ID type / visa / dependents🟡Dependents feed nothing yet; visa feeds SPT exempt-status only loosely. → Residency detail + credits.
Green card: grant date, I-407 surrender, exit tax (§877A), years-held❌Residency treats green-card = resident, but expatriation / exit tax is not detected. → Residency + a new critical Conflict.
Substantial Presence Test — 3-year day tracker, excluded days, exempt individual🟡SPT result (resident/not) drives residency; the Monitor shows the current-year day-counter. The weighted 3-year formula, excluded days, exempt-individual are read on the form but the engine uses the pre-computed status, not the raw calc. → monitoring day-counter + computation residency.
First-year election / dual-status year❌→ Residency + computation.
DTAA Article 4 tie-breaker wizard✅Residency tab (DTAA Treaty Position) + Monitor conflict ("resolved / not run"); applied end-to-end in computation (loser → source basis).
Treaty docs: TRC, Form 8833, 1040-NR✅Residency (TRC / 10F / 1040-NR rows) + Documents + "treaty relief without docs" conflict.

2. State residency & multi-state

Layer 1 stepStatusWhere
Domicile, year-start/end home, states-lived list❌The US-states map is illustrative only — it is not computed from these fields. → new state-residency engine feeding the US drill-down map.
Statutory residency (NY 183-day + abode, CA FTB domicile/exit)❌Same — needs the state engine. → Monitor US drill-down + Conflicts.
Multi-state apportionment, economic/sales nexus, gross receipts by state❌→ state-residency + business-nexus engine.
Military / duty-station, community-property state❌→ state engine.
State tax is the single biggest gap. Today the state map is a demo visual; making it live is "Phase: US-state residency."

3. Employment income (W-2)

Layer 1StatusWhere
W-2 wages / paystubs (+ withholding, Medicare wages)✅Holdings → US income by head (Wages); computation taxes it; withholding feeds the calendar/FTC.

4. Investments & capital gains (1099-B)

Layer 1StatusWhere
Short-term / long-term gains, proceeds, cost basis✅Holdings (US income by head — ST/LT gains); computation applies ordinary vs LTCG 0/15/20 + the cross-basis re-characterization.
RSU cost-basis warning❌→ Conflicts (RSU basis trap) + Equity-comp coverage (§9).
QSBS §1202 exclusion❌→ computation (gain exclusion) + Conflicts.
Real-estate sale §121 exclusion❌→ computation + Conflicts.
Collectibles 28% rate❌→ computation special-rate.
Capital-loss carryovers (ST/LT)❌→ computation (offset gains).
Crypto / VDA transactions❌→ Holdings (asset class) + computation (gains) + Conflicts (reporting).

5. Interest & dividends (Schedule B)

Layer 1StatusWhere
US bank interest, ordinary & qualified dividends✅Holdings (US income by head); computation (ordinary + qualified-rate split).
Treasury/OID, seller-financed, tax-exempt interest🟡Plain interest covered; OID / tax-exempt / muni not separated (tax-exempt should be excluded + feed AMT/NIIT). → normalize + computation.

6. Rental income (Schedule E)

Layer 1StatusWhere
Gross US rental + expenses✅Holdings (US income by head — Rental; Property card); computation + cross-basis (US depreciation vs India 30%). Depreciation is currently modeled, not from a basis schedule.

7. Other income (Schedule 1 / 1099-G / SSA)

Layer 1StatusWhere
Social Security (SSA-1099)🟡Read into US retirement income (usRetirementIncome) as ordinary; the 85% taxable cap is not applied. → computation.
State/local refunds, unemployment, alimony, royalties, COD (1099-C), HSA distributions, gambling/misc❌→ normalize (Other-income head) + Holdings + computation.

8. Business & self-employment

Layer 1StatusWhere
Schedule C, K-1 (1065 / 1120-S / 1041), 1120 C-Corp🟡Amounts flow into businessUs; C-Corp taxed 21%, pass-through $0-at-entity. Sch F (farm), trust K-1 partially. Holdings shows business income.
SE tax (Schedule SE, 15.3%)❌Self-employment income is taxed at income rates but SE tax is not added. → computation (material gap for the self-employed).
QBI §199A 20% deduction❌→ computation + Conflicts (SSTB/limits).
SE above-the-line (SE health, Solo-401k/SEP)🟡Retirement contributions captured in Holdings; the deduction isn't applied to AGI. → computation.
Balance sheet / Sch L, M-1, M-2 (book-tax reconciliation)❌→ computation (C-Corp) + a Business detail panel.
Depreciation (MACRS / §179 / bonus), UBIA❌→ computation (business net) + cross-basis rental.
Foreign branch / FDE (Form 8858), branch K-1❌→ International (§11) + Documents.

9. Equity & cap table (RSU / ISO / NSO / ESPP / QSBS)

Layer 1StatusWhere
RSU vests, ISO/NSO exercises, ESPP, restricted stock❌Entirely uncovered — ISO exercise is a top AMT trigger. → Holdings (equity comp) + computation (ordinary + AMT) + Conflicts.

10. International income & assets

Layer 1StatusWhere
Foreign wages / interest / dividends / ST-LT CG / rental / pension✅Holdings (foreign heads) + cross-basis + FTC (Filings). Core of the India⇄US value prop.
Section 988 currency gains/losses❌→ computation + Conflicts.
FEIE (Form 2555) — tax home, bona-fide / physical-presence tests, housing✅Eligibility gated (Monitor conflict "FEIE claimed but not eligible" + applied in computation with §911 no-double-dip). Housing exclusion not yet modelled.
FBAR peak balance✅Accounts (reporting limits) + Monitor conflict (breach) + Documents (FinCEN 114).
Form 8938 (FATCA)✅Accounts + Documents; threshold table now keys off the §911 abroad facts.
Foreign bank accounts list✅Accounts (Foreign Accounts) — drives FBAR / Schedule FA.
Other financial assets (FATCA / PFIC)✅Holdings (Securities, PFIC · 8621 tag) + Monitor conflict + Documents (8621).
Form 5471 (CFC / GILTI / Subpart F)✅Monitor conflict (definitive from ownership) + Holdings (CFC tag + GILTI) + Documents + cross-basis routing.
Form 8865 (foreign partnership), 8858 (FDE), 5472 (inbound)❌Read on the form; not surfaced. → Documents + Conflicts.
Foreign gifts / trusts (Form 3520)🟡3520 fires for Indian retirement (PPF/EPF); foreign gifts 3520 not. → Conflicts + Documents.
Treaty FDAP rates, ECI/FDAP classification, FIRPTA withholding❌→ computation (FDAP/ECI split) + Conflicts (FIRPTA on US real-estate sale by NRA).

11. Retirement

Layer 1StatusWhere
US: Traditional/Roth IRA, 401(k) employee/match, Roth 401(k), HSA, Solo-401k, SEP✅Holdings → Retirement Accounts (this-year contributions). The deduction to AGI isn't applied yet → computation.
IRA / 401(k) distributions, pension✅usRetirementIncome → ordinary income (Holdings + computation).
Indian retirement (US tax exposure): trust review, 3520 flag✅Monitor conflict ("EPF/PPF/NPS taxed differently by the US") + Holdings + Documents (3520).
SECURE Act 2.0 RMD❌→ computation (forced income) + Conflicts (missed-RMD penalty).

12. Deductions & credits

Layer 1StatusWhere
Standard vs itemized; SALT (capped), mortgage interest, charity, medical🟡computation applies standard/itemized incl. SALT cap. Mortgage acquisition-date limit, appreciated-asset charity, disaster loss not refined.
Credits: Child/Dependent Care, AOTC, Lifetime Learning, 529❌No credits are applied. → computation (credits reduce tax) + a Filings credits panel.

13. AMT (§55)

Layer 1StatusWhere
AMT preference adjustments, private-activity-bond interest❌AMT is not computed at all — a real gap given ISO exercises + high earners. → computation (parallel AMT) + Conflicts.

14. NIIT (§1411)

Layer 1StatusWhere
Net Investment Income Tax 3.8%✅computation (NIIT on investment income over the threshold) → Filings tax computation.

15. Foreign Tax Credit (§904)

Layer 1StatusWhere
FTC baskets, foreign taxes✅Filings → FTC Reconciliation (Form 1116 limitation, carryover, residual) + cross-basis. Per-basket (passive vs general) limitation is simplified to one basket. → computation refinement.

16. Estimated tax / safe harbor

Layer 1StatusWhere
1040-ES quarterly, prior-year tax (110%/90% safe harbor)🟡The deadlines appear in the Filings compliance calendar; the underpayment / safe-harbor calc is not done. → computation + calendar.

17. Additional Medicare (§3101(b)(2))

Layer 1StatusWhere
Additional Medicare 0.9%🟡computation uses the form-provided figure rather than computing it from wages/SE. → computation.

Scorecard

BucketCountExamples
✅ Covered~18Residency + Art.4 tie-breaker, W-2, cap gains, interest/dividends, rental, foreign income, FEIE gate, FBAR, 8938, PFIC/8621, CFC/5471/GILTI, FTC/1116, NIIT, US + Indian retirement, cross-basis
🟡 Partial~10Filing-status/NRA rules, SPT raw calc, business (no SE tax/QBI), Social Security 85%, deductions, FTC baskets, estimated-tax calc, Add'l Medicare
❌ Not yet~20State tax (biggest), AMT, equity comp/ISO, SE tax, QBI §199A, credits (AOTC/care/529), crypto/§988, QSBS/§121/collectibles/loss-carryovers, expatriation/exit tax, FIRPTA, 8865/8858/5472, foreign-gift 3520, RMD

Recommended fill order (highest impact first)

  1. SE tax + QBI §199A — anyone self-employed is currently mis-taxed. (computation)
  2. AMT — needed the moment ISO/equity-comp or high earners appear. (computation)
  3. Credits (child care, AOTC, LLC, 529) — directly change tax owed. (computation)
  4. Equity comp (RSU/ISO/ESPP) — income + AMT + a Holdings panel.
  5. US-state residency engine — makes the state map real (NY/CA statutory tests, nexus).
  6. Capital-gains specials (QSBS/§121/collectibles/loss carryovers, crypto/§988).
  7. Expatriation/exit tax, FIRPTA, 8865/8858/5472, foreign-gift 3520 — completeness of the international suite.
